| **Which storage contract controls complete-source capacity planning** | **`e0-compact-evidence/2`, and the `132.5 GB` figure is retired from planning — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §5 (D124-R2).** The two figures are **successive evidence contracts, not contradictory predictions of one implementation**: Decision 112 §6's about-`132.5 GB` is `e0-compact-evidence/1`, whose **resolution layer is still uncompacted**, while [Decision 113](Decisions/decision_113_m3_3_compact_derived_e0_evidence.md) §15 and §19 `e0-compact-evidence/2` **additionally compacts resolution and derived evidence** — smaller because the contract **writes less**, not because the estimate was revised. `/2`, calibrated by [Decision 118](Decisions/decision_118_m3_3_read_only_performance_diagnosis.md) and by the later real D120/D122/D123 evidence, controls. **Decision 112 is not retired as historical evidence** — only its use as *current planning capacity* is, and `src/disclosure_drift/m3/capacity_plan.py` stays untouched. This closes the Decision 123 §9.2 conflict |
| **What fraction of accessions carried explicit resolution in the F1 pass** | **About `7.73%` — and the `61.8%` reading is a published error, not a finding — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §7 (D124-R4).** Over [Decision 122](Decisions/decision_122_m3_3_d120_f1_finalization_characterization.md) §5's `8,258,521` accessions, `638,071 / 8,258,521 = 0.077262` is the **explicit-accession fraction**, about **`7.73%`**; `5,104,568 / 8,258,521 = 0.618097` is **field-resolution ROWS per accession**, because one explicit accession can emit **multiple** field rows. The D124 technical review read the second as the first — wrong by roughly a factor of eight. **Three prohibitions follow**: do **not** publish that `61.8%` of accessions were explicit; do **not** claim the tail is proven to become more implicit; and do **not** characterize linear F1 byte extrapolation as necessarily **"conservative-high"** on that basis. The linear F1 central projection **survives** because it rests on **measured bytes per accession**, never on the resolution mix. A term-label condition is recorded rather than resolved: D122 §5 labels `638,071` **cohort-resolution rows** while D124 reads it as explicit-resolution accessions, and **both quotients hold under either reading** |
| **What a complete first source is estimated to cost** | **About `75.27 GiB` at the central peak, on planning estimates rather than observation — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §8.** Materialization durable state about `54.62 GB`; compact sidecar about `0.27 GB`; F1 durable growth about `9.40 GB` at about `1.33 h` with bounded log and resident set; F2 durable growth about `6.59 GB` with a pre-checkpoint/pre-truncation write-ahead log of about `9.52 GB`, about `2.04 h` wall, and about `0.68 GiB` resident set; central peak about **`75.27 GiB`** and conservative modeled peak about **`89.27 GiB`**. **Every figure is a planning estimate, not a complete-source observation** — the complete first source has never been run. **Scope is the complete FIRST SOURCE only and this is NOT 76-source E0 capacity evidence**, per the [Decision 121](Decisions/decision_121_m3_3_finalization_feasibility_preflight.md) §5 limit. A **unit condition** is stated rather than converted: components are in `GB` and peaks in `GiB` |
| **What must be free before a complete-source run may start** | **`>= 105 GiB` = `112,742,891,520` bytes — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §9 (D124-R5).** The owner ruling **supersedes the reviewer's recommended `100 GiB` minimum**, adding margin for corrected F1 uncertainty, the F2 `26x` extrapolation, APFS/background drift, and unmeasured SQLite temporary-space behaviour. About `110 GiB` is **preferable headroom, not a second authorization predicate**. **In flight**: a continuous hard-stop floor of **`10 GiB`**, and **`>= 30 GiB` measured immediately before opening F2** — **below that, F2 must not begin**. **No `VACUUM`**, and SQLite temporary storage must be **explicitly placed and accounted** on the internal run volume. **The Decision 121 §7 `>= 85 GiB` floor is SUPERSEDED, not merely suspended** — Decision 123 §9.2 (R74) suspended it pending this reconciliation, and D124-R5 replaces it |
| **Whether the external SSD may be used, and for what** | **Archival storage in principle only — never as the active governed SQLite volume — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §10 and §13 (D124-R6).** The read-only discovery: `SSK SSD`, `500 GB` nominal, about `499.94 GB` / `465.61 GiB` free at observation, USB SuperSpeed+ at about `10 Gbps` visible, filesystem **exFAT**, with a user-reported sequential capability of about `1050 MB/s` that **was not independently benchmarked**. **Active SQLite use is NOT approved**, and **reformatting is neither approved nor required**. **No categorical claim is made about whether SQLite can technically operate on exFAT** — the governing fact is simpler: the external SSD is **not needed and not approved** as the active governed volume, Plan B is unnecessary and **NOT APPROVED**, and the **internal APFS Apple SSD remains the intended active working volume** |
| **What must happen before a preserved world may be deleted** | **External verification first, and a fresh hash before anything goes — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §11 (D124-R7, R8, R9).** D117, D120, D122, the D123 characterization residue, and disposable performance material become eligible **under a later archival instrument** — **eligibility is not authorization**, and **only after external verification may authorized internal copies be removed**. C3's retained about-`10 MiB` evidence and tiny diagnostics **stay internal**. The D122 post-F1 catalog **requires a fresh SHA-256**: the historical candidate `fa4a635d…9413` from the D123 completion report is **not yet accepted durable identity**, so the instrument must **hash the actual preserved catalog, compare, STOP on mismatch, and record the resulting identity before deletion**. Because **exFAT does not preserve all relevant POSIX metadata**, the external archive must carry an explicit cryptographic/metadata manifest and/or a metadata-preserving container covering relative paths, file types, exact bytes, SHA-256, mode, uid/gid or owner/group, run/world ID, repository HEAD and tree, destination, and verification result |
| **Whether archival alone opens a complete-source run** | **No — it leaves about a `6.54 GiB` gap — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §12 and §14 (D124-R10).** The clone-aware internal reclaim estimate is about **`61.77 GiB`** against about **`36.70 GiB`** currently free, projecting to about **`98.46 GiB`** — which **does not satisfy the `105 GiB` gate**. The owner's preference is to free or move **ordinary non-project data** later rather than alter append-only project raw evidence to close a gap this small, and **no non-project deletion is authorized or performed**. Before any complete-source authorization: **archival must complete; authorized internal copies must be reclaimed; internal free space must be MEASURED at `>= 105 GiB`** — measured, not projected — **and all other preflight predicates must pass**. **E0 remains NOT AUTHORIZED**, and the D124 observations about possible 76-source storage are **NON-GOVERNING** |
| **How to cite a ruling number now that two records issue R70–R78** | **Decision-qualified, always — and new rulings are decision-scoped — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §3 (D124-G1, G2, G3).** [Decision 094](Decisions/decision_094_m3_3_pre_e0_executability_redesign.md) R70–R78 and Decision 123 R70–R78 are **both historically immutable**, and **neither is renumbered** (**D124-G1**). Every future reference to a colliding ID must **name its decision** — "Decision 094 R72" or "Decision 123 R72", never a bare `R72` (**D124-G2**). From Decision 124 onward, new owner rulings use **decision-scoped IDs** — `D124-R1`, `D124-R2`, and so on (**D124-G3**). The collision class is **closed going forward rather than repaired backward**, which is what the Decision 123 §10 numbering condition deferred to D124 |
| **Whether the D122 post-F1 working-catalog identity is settled** | **Yes — it is ACCEPTED durable identity, no longer a candidate — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §3 (D125-R2).** The value is `fa4a635d36a487774e02670bb0fab1ded1c696b5e25faf54fb6f55b69799f413`. [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §11 (D124-R8) required the archival instrument to **hash the actual preserved catalog, compare against the historical D123 completion-report candidate, STOP ON MISMATCH, and record the identity BEFORE any deletion** — because a completion report is not repository authority. **Four independent reads converged**: a fresh internal D125 hash, the historical D123 candidate, the **external archive member streamed back off the external device and rehashed**, and multiple independent reads. **The stop-on-mismatch condition never fired**, and the D124-R8 obligation is discharged |
| **Where the retired M3.3 evidence now lives, and how anyone knows it arrived intact** | **On the external `SSK SSD` as seven verified PAX/TAR archives — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §§4–5.** Destination `/Volumes/SSK SSD/FDD_M3_D125_ARCHIVE/`, uncompressed **PAX/TAR**, filesystem **exFAT**: `d117_world.tar` `27,629,187,072` B, `d120_world.tar` `19,955,783,680` B, `d122_f1_world.tar` `23,530,204,160` B, `d123_characterization.tar` `4,034,596,352` B, `m3_perf_experiments.tar` `84,995,072` B, `c3_retained_evidence.tar` `11,043,328` B, and `d117_diagnostics.tar` `277,504` B — **`75,246,087,168`** bytes total, about **`70.0784 GiB`**, each with a recorded SHA-256. **Verification streamed the actual TAR contents BACK from the external device** rather than re-stating what the writer believed it had written: **`2,777`/`2,777`** members matched, **`2,470`/`2,470`** regular-file SHA-256 values matched, `0` missing, `0` unexpected, `0` duplicate, and **all metadata comparisons passed**. The two counts differ because directories and symlinks are verified for presence and metadata but carry no content hash |
| **How the archive survives exFAT's metadata limitations** | **PAX metadata plus three independent records — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §6 (satisfying D124-R9).** The archive does **not** rely on the filesystem to carry POSIX metadata. Four mechanisms carry it: **PAX archive metadata** in the container; **explicit manifests** independent of the container; **per-file hashes**; and recorded **`mode`, `uid`/`gid`, `owner`/`group`, `path`, `type`, `size`, `mtime`, and symlink metadata**. The permanent internal retention root is `~/m3-retention/d125/`, the primary final manifest is `d125_manifest_final.json` at SHA-256 `1366a5dd…3a9c`, and **the external RETENTION copy was verified byte-identical to the internal record**, so the manifest survives the loss of either side. **The manifest is the authority for metadata exFAT itself cannot hold** |
| **Which internal roots were retired, and in what order relative to verification** | **Exactly five, and ONLY AFTER external verification passed — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §7.** Removed: the D117, D120, D122-F1, and D123 canary worlds under `~/m3-disposable-canaries/` (`m3_3_d117_first_source_disposable_v1`, `m3_3_d120_cache_120k_prefix_v1`, `m3_3_d122_d120_f1_finalization_v1`, `m3_3_d123_f2_characterization_v1`) plus `~/m3-perf-experiments`. **The ordering is the governance point, not an implementation detail** — [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §11 (D124-R7) permitted removal **only after external verification**, and that is the order that occurred: hash, archive, stream back, verify, record the identity and manifests, and only then retire. **The verified external archives now stand as the preserved evidence.** Nothing else was removed, and **no further deletion is authorized** (D125-R3) |
| **What Disclosure Drift material is still held internally** | **The private evidence root, the C3 world, the diagnostics, the retention root, and the live project — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §8.** Protected: `~/m3-private-evidence`, `~/m3-disposable-canaries/m3_3_perf_c3_local_40k_v1`, `~/m3-d117-diagnostics`, and `~/m3-retention/d125`, together with **the repository**, **the current raw SEC source**, and **the operational catalog**. This satisfies the [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §11 requirement that C3's retained evidence and the tiny diagnostics stay internal; the raw SEC source is append-only under the standing repository rule and was never in scope for archival or deletion |
| **How much space the reclamation actually recovered, and whether the model held** | **`61.7365 GiB`, and the clone-aware model held to about `0.03 GiB` — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §9.** Measured reclamation **`66,289,078,272`** bytes = **`61.7365 GiB`**, against the [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §12 clone-aware prediction of about **`61.77 GiB`**. **That is the one genuinely predictive result in the record**: an estimate made over APFS copy-on-write extents *before* the deletion landed within about `0.03 GiB` of the measured free-block change. Reclamation is measured from **statvfs free-block change, not from `du`** — D120 and D122 shared APFS extents, so per-target deltas are **not** additive with apparent file sizes |
| **Whether the complete-source free-space gate is now met** | **No — short by about `7.68 GiB` — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §9 (D125-R5).** Settled internal free after D125 is **`104,500,830,208`** bytes = **`97.3240 GiB`**, against the controlling D124-R5 starting gate of `112,742,891,520` bytes = `105 GiB`, leaving a **remaining hard gap of `8,242,061,312` bytes = `7.6760 GiB`**. **The gate stands unchanged and unrelaxed.** A **measurement condition** is stated rather than smoothed: free space on a live APFS volume drifts, and three readings spread about `17 MB` (about `0.016%`) — the settled figure, the D125 retention artifact's `104,503,115,776` bytes, and a publication-time re-read of `104,485,810,176` bytes. **All three fall short by about the same `7.68 GiB`, so the conclusion is identical under any of them** |
| **Whether `110 GiB` is a second gate** | **No — it is an owner PREFERENCE and must never be described as the formal gate — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §9 (D125-R6).** The owner prefers staging approximately `110 GiB` free **if ordinary non-project cleanup makes that easy**, which would need about `12.7 GiB` more than the settled `97.3240 GiB` baseline. **The formal admission predicate remains `>= 105 GiB`** = `112,742,891,520` bytes (D124-R5). [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §9 already said the same of the same figure — *preferable headroom, not a second authorization predicate* |
| **Whether crossing the disk threshold authorizes a complete-source run** | **No — a final live preflight is still required — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §13 (D125-R7).** Disk space was one blocking predicate, not the whole predicate set. **Crossing the threshold does not itself authorize execution**: a final live M3.3 complete-source preflight must still run and every other predicate must still pass. A complete-source run, E0-v3, F1, F2, the full-population F2 rerun, the D117 retry, the three-source canary, the real replay proof, migration `0016`, network, and acquisition all remain unauthorized |
| **What the leftover E0 namespaces and the stale lease mean** | **They are historical residue, NOT a new E0 namespace — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §10 (D125-R8).** Two **consumed** namespaces — `m3_3_e0_offline_parse_v1` (interrupted) and `m3_3_e0_offline_parse_v2` (consumed) — remain under the private evidence root, alongside a stale `catalog_writer.lease`. **D125 did not modify any of them**: not archived, not deleted, not touched. **Their mere existence must never be characterized as a new E0 namespace** — a consumed namespace is spent, not available, and **no E0-v3 authority exists**. The operational consequence of leaving them in place is that **they must be explicitly checked during the final pre-complete-source safety preflight** rather than discovered by it. Two idle historical `tmux` servers were also observed and held no project handles |
| **Whether the stored-object naming question is closed** | **No — it is carried forward, explicitly not discharged — [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §12.** [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §4 recorded that `c85744be921b0dc5` appears in **no tracked record before Decision 124**, while [Decision 059](Decisions/decision_059_m3_2_orphan_adoption_final_acceptance_m3_l16_closure_and_governance_synchronization.md) §3 names the older object, and it named reconciling that **stored-object naming** as a D125 obligation. **The D125 owner instrument is scoped to archival and reclamation and does not address it**, so it is **recorded as outstanding rather than silently treated as closed**. **No reader may cite D125 as having closed it.** The [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §7 term-label condition — D122 §5 labels `638,071` cohort-resolution rows while D124 reads it as explicit-resolution accessions — likewise stands as recorded there |
| **Whether the final live complete-source preflight has run, and what it returned** | **It ran, and it returned `NOT_READY_IMPLEMENTATION_GAP` — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §1.** This is the preflight [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §13 (D125-R7) made a precondition of any complete-source authorization, and the record is **retrospective**: the preflight had already run when the file was written, and the file did not authorize it. **The shape of the verdict matters more than the word `NOT_READY`, and four things must be read together**: it is **NOT a live-state failure**; **every live-state predicate PASSED**; complete-source execution **did not occur**; and it **remains unauthorized**. The blocker is a **missing guard in this repository's own source**, not a condition of the machine |
| **Whether the complete-source free-space gate is met now** | **Yes, with room — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §4 (D126-R2).** Entry free **`137,633,968,128`** bytes = **`128.1816 GiB`**; exit free **`136,576,831,488`** bytes = **`127.1971 GiB`**; the preflight itself consumed `1,057,136,640` bytes = `0.9845 GiB`. **Both readings clear both levels**: the formal [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §9 (D124-R5) gate of `112,742,891,520` bytes = `105 GiB`, and the D125-R6 owner *preference* of `118,111,600,640` bytes = `110 GiB` — exit stands `23,833,939,968` bytes = `22.1971 GiB` above the gate. **The [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §9 shortfall is closed by measurement**: entry free is `33,133,137,920` bytes = `30.8576 GiB` above D125's settled `97.3240 GiB`, closed from ordinary non-project storage with **no further Disclosure Drift evidence deleted**. `0 GiB` of material stale pytest scratch remained. A **measurement condition** is published rather than smoothed: a publication-time re-read returned `136,511,447,040` bytes = `127.1362 GiB`, about `65 MB` below the exit figure — **all three readings clear both levels, so the conclusion is identical under any of them** |
| **What the preflight measured about the frozen source and the operational catalog** | **Every identity matched what governance already recorded — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §§4.2–4.3.** Source `sec_bulk_submissions-c85744be921b0dc5.zip`: `1,556,847,020` bytes, SHA-256 `c85744be921b0dc5be4e3c7dd44552fc0f57d354d61df38cd92a13926982b82f`, **`985,835` raw ZIP entries**, **`985,834` governed JSON members**, and **exactly one** non-JSON entry — the same `placeholder.txt` [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §4 recorded, which is the whole of the `985,835`/`985,834` difference. Catalog: file SHA-256 `57e36a78…0af2ded`, logical digest `5c823d21…6ad306`, observation-set digest `b1122bb9…8e533be`, **migration head `15`**, **`76` plan sources**, **all parser states `not_started`**, and **zero** E0 parser/accession/record materialization residue. `76` sources all `not_started` with zero residue together mean the complete-source path would **start from a clean state, not resume into one** |
| **Whether the stored-object naming question is closed** | **Yes — it is discharged — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §5 (D126-R4).** This closes what [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §4 opened and [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §12 explicitly carried forward. **The runtime authority is the pointer, not the prose**: selection reads the **single-valued** `census_plan_sources.observation_id` `09fdacf651ba4d0a80f5d2ab4e36f4a3` together with its `985,834` governed member rows — one pointer, one archive, one member count. **[Decision 059](Decisions/decision_059_m3_2_orphan_adoption_final_acceptance_m3_l16_closure_and_governance_synchronization.md) §3 remains historically correct and is NOT amended**: it named the object stored *then*, and a record that correctly describes an earlier state is not corrected by a later state arriving. One confirming property is recorded because it makes the naming **self-verifying**: the identifier `c85744be921b0dc5` is the **first sixteen hexadecimal characters of the object's own SHA-256**, so the pointer and the bytes verify each other without reference to any document |
| **What the missing supersession edge between the two source observations means** | **A nonblocking documentation limitation, recorded rather than repaired — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §5 (D126-R4).** The controlling August-11 observation carries **no explicit `supersedes_observation_id` edge** to the August-07 one, so the supersession is true in substance and unrecorded as a link. **It does not affect runtime correctness**, because selection reads the single-valued pointer and **never walks a supersession chain**. **It authorizes no catalog write** — it is recorded as a limitation precisely so that it is *not* repaired by opening a writer against the operational catalog to add an edge |
| **What the leftover E0 namespaces and the lease mean at the final preflight** | **Historical residue, and the lease is `released` rather than stale-`held` — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §6 (D126-R3).** This is the [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §10 (D125-R8) residue check, performed as required. `m3_3_e0_offline_parse_v1` and `m3_3_e0_offline_parse_v2` remain present and **consumed**, **no `v3` exists**, and both are **structurally irrelevant to the canary path**, which neither reads nor writes them. The lease records `state` **`released`**, `lease_id` `3a37a7ee0e0c4496a4fba845d4d5d2a1`, `writer_pid` `68482` **dead**, `released_at_utc` `2026-08-18T01:44:33.497032Z`, file SHA-256 `8c0f251f…19db29b`, and the **advisory lock was available**. **The former stale-*held* condition — the one Decisions 102 through 107 were built to reconcile — is ABSENT.** No recovery authority and no recovery action is required, `STALE_WRITER_LEASE_RECOVERY_AUTHORITY` stays `None`, and **the file must not be modified**: a `released` lease is the correct terminal state of a writer that exited properly, not a defect to clean up |
| **What actually blocks the complete-source run, now that live state passes** | **One missing line of production code — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §7.** The path runs F1 `count_persisted_accession_resolutions(...)` and then F2 `materialize_census_associations(...)` as the **immediately following statement**, with **no `>= 30 GiB` disk admission predicate between F1's return and F2's transaction opening** — which [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §9 (D124-R5) requires taken *immediately before* opening F2 and explicitly **not** inherited from the starting gate. **The gate is not weakened by being unmet; that is what makes it the blocker** |
| **Why an external sampler cannot enforce the pre-F2 gate** | **Four independent reasons, each sufficient alone — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §7.** **(1)** No enforceable pause exists at the boundary — F1 returns and F2 begins in consecutive statements, so there is no window an outside process can occupy. **(2)** Ledger state does not distinguish F1 from impending F2 — nothing durable changes there, so an observer cannot tell the two apart by reading state. **(3)** An external process can **signal** but cannot **decline admission atomically** — a signal is advisory where the predicate must be dispositive. **(4)** A sampling race survives any cadence — free space measured at any instant before the call describes a different instant than the one that matters; tightening the interval shrinks the race and **never closes it**. **Only the path about to open the transaction can decline to open it** |
| **How the other four D124-R5 safety requirements are satisfied** | **Without touching production code, which is why D127's scope is one guard — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §8 (D126-R5).** Start `>= 105 GiB` is **launch-wrapper enforceable**; the continuous `10 GiB` floor through interruptible phases is **monitor/wrapper enforceable**; **no `VACUUM`** is **already true by construction** — the path issues none; and explicit SQLite temporary placement is **wrapper-enforceable with `SQLITE_TMPDIR`**. **The temp directory is a *sibling*** — `<work-root>/<run-id>__sqlite_tmp/` and expressly **not** `<world>/tmp` — **because precreating `<world>/tmp` violates the world's create-once semantics**: a wrapper that materializes a directory inside the world beforehand has already written into a world that is supposed to be created rather than found. A sibling puts the temporary space on the same volume without touching the world at all |
| **Whether a complete-source run may be killed on a clock** | **No — there is no arbitrary wall-clock ceiling — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §9 (D126-R7).** The `~15 h` optimistic / `~24 h` central / `~34 h+` possible figures are a **planning envelope, not a deadline**; **actual health, progress, and resource monitoring controls**. **The run is not resumable**, so elapsed time alone is **not a valid kill criterion**: killing a healthy run at an arbitrary hour destroys all of its work and buys nothing, while a genuinely unhealthy run is identifiable from progress and resource behaviour long before any clock threshold fires. AC power was confirmed; a future run needs an explicit **per-process** no-sleep assertion — `caffeinate -i -m -s` — against the current `sleep=1`/`disksleep=10` settings, and **persistent `pmset` settings must not be altered** |
| **Whether the proposed complete-source command is frozen** | **No — it is expressly not frozen — [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §10 (D126-R8).** The proposed run ID `m3_3_d126_complete_first_source_v1` and its command were **not executed** and are **not frozen for eventual execution**, because **D127 changes the published code baseline** — a frozen command identifies a run against a specific tree, and freezing one would name a tree about to stop existing. The final run identity and command must be **regenerated** after **both** D127 implementation acceptance **and** a new final live preflight. **Nothing in Decision 126 may be cited as a frozen command contract** |
| **What closes the pre-F2 gap, and where it lives** | **One constant and one guard in the canary path — [Decision 127](Decisions/decision_127_m3_3_pre_f2_admission_guard.md) §2.** `PRE_F2_MINIMUM_FREE_BYTES = 30 * 1024**3` = **`32,212,254,720`** bytes, exported in `__all__`, and `_require_pre_f2_free_space()` measuring `shutil.disk_usage(...).free` on the disposable world's own volume — `working.path.parent`, the directory holding the working catalog and its write-ahead log, which is the storage F2's transaction actually consumes. The call sits **between** F1 and F2 in `_materialize`, and the [Decision 094](Decisions/decision_094_m3_3_pre_e0_executability_redesign.md) §6.4 resolution-before-projection ordering is **unchanged** — the guard is inserted between them and reorders nothing. **It is an admission predicate, not a budget**: it moves no row, no ordering, no digest, and no identity |
| **What the pre-F2 guard does when the floor is not met** | **It refuses before F2's transaction opens, and says so — [Decision 127](Decisions/decision_127_m3_3_pre_f2_admission_guard.md) §2.2.** The comparison is a **strict `<`**, so the floor itself **admits**: `>= 30 GiB` is the accepted rule and exactly `30 GiB` is inside it. The refusal raises `SingleSourceCanaryError` — the module's existing precondition failure, *never worked around, never retried in place* — and its message names the **actual free bytes**, the **required minimum**, and that **F2 was refused before its single transaction opened**. It names **no path**, in keeping with the rest of the module, and [Decision 116](Decisions/decision_116_m3_3_disposable_single_source_canary_path.md) §10 carries through the new gate: the accepted catalog is byte-identical after the refusal |
| **How the pre-F2 guard is proved, and proved to be non-vacuous** | **Six passing tests plus two mutations — [Decision 127](Decisions/decision_127_m3_3_pre_f2_admission_guard.md) §3.** `tests/unit/test_d127_pre_f2_admission_guard.py` pins the constant to its literal byte value; refuses one byte below the floor with the full message contract; runs a **real end-to-end run below the floor in which F2 is replaced by a tripwire that records nothing**; admits at **exactly** the floor and one byte above; and proves ordering from a real run's call log of `["F1", "GUARD", "F2"]` written by the three **real** participants, each wrapped rather than replaced. **Non-vacuity is proved by mutation, because a test that passes against a missing guard proves nothing**: deleting the guard call produces **2 failures** and weakening `<` to `<=` produces **1** — both applied to the working tree, measured, and reverted. Free space is **pinned in every test**, which is sound because the guard's comparison is the **only** free-space comparison in the module — every other `disk_usage` call there records a number and gates nothing |
| **What the pre-F2 guard costs, stated rather than smoothed** | **The synthetic canary suites now also have to clear a `30 GiB` machine floor — [Decision 127](Decisions/decision_127_m3_3_pre_f2_admission_guard.md) §5.** The guard applies to **every** run of the path, including the Decision 116 and Decision 119 three-member synthetic worlds that need kilobytes, so **on a machine with less than `30 GiB` free those suites would fail at the admission gate** rather than at anything they test. **This is a consequence of the authorized scope, not a defect in it**: making the floor injectable or test-overridable is scope expansion, and a test-overridable safety floor is a weaker artifact than D124-R5 asked for. It is recorded so a later reader meets it as a **known disposition rather than a mystery**. The exposure is bounded — the project already requires `>= 105 GiB` before a complete-source run, and Decision 126 §4 measured `127.1971 GiB` free |
| **Whether the pre-F2 guard opens the complete-source run** | **No — closing the gap is not opening the run — [Decision 127](Decisions/decision_127_m3_3_pre_f2_admission_guard.md) §7.** Decision 127 is **`READY FOR INDEPENDENT REVIEW` and NOT owner-accepted**, and emits no owner-acceptance token. **Independent review comes first**, then owner acceptance, and only then the two things [Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) §10 (D126-R8) requires before any run: a **regenerated run identity and command**, and a **new final live preflight**. The D126 run identity is **not revived** — D126-R8 retired it precisely because this change was coming. **It relaxes no gate**: the `105 GiB` start gate, the continuous `10 GiB` floor, no-`VACUUM`, and explicit `SQLITE_TMPDIR` placement all stand where Decisions 124 §9 and 126 §8 leave them. All three activation constants remain `None`, migration head remains `0015` with `0016` absent, and both tracked network switches remain `false` at request ceiling `0`. **Complete source is NOT authorized. E0 is NOT authorized** |
| **Whether the complete-source run has now happened** | **Yes — D128 ran to completion, and its result is PARTIALLY accepted — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §1.** The complete-first-source canary `m3_3_d128_complete_first_source_v1` traversed the whole governed `985,834`-member source and finished, over a `33 h 18 m 43 s` wall run. **Decision 129 is a retrospective record**: it did not exist before the run and did not authorize it, and the authorizing instrument was the owner instrument issued at the time, which this repository does not hold. **The disposition is partial, and the partition is the point — D128 is accepted as an EXECUTION and rejected as a MEASUREMENT.** The outcome token is `D128_SEMANTIC_REPAIR_REQUIRED`. |
| **Where Decision 128 is** | **There is no Decision 128, and there never was — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §2.** `D128` names a **run identity**, not a decision record: the disposable canary world `m3_3_d128_complete_first_source_v1`. The registry index therefore steps from `127` to `129`, and the gap is **expected rather than missing** — the same shape as the Decision 102 gap the registry already carries as an open item. **Decision 129 is the first and only governance record of D128.** |
| **What D128 is accepted for** | **Its execution mechanics, and nothing about its counts — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §3 (D129-R1).** Accepted independently: the full `985,834`-member complete-source traversal mechanics; exactly-one-source execution mechanics; the runtime and resource evidence; bounded-memory feasibility at peak RSS `3,178,020,864` bytes = `2.9598 GiB`; **F1 feasibility**; **F2 feasibility**; the [Decision 127](Decisions/decision_127_m3_3_pre_f2_admission_guard.md) pre-F2 admission gate's **operation** — its first exercise in a real run, and it **admitted**; complete working-catalog finalization; the write-ahead-log checkpoint to **zero**; **operational-catalog nonmutation**; the monitor and runtime evidence; and **D128 itself as a valid negative semantic experiment**. **Feasibility is not correctness**: every item is a statement about whether the path can run, not about what it recorded. |
| **What D128 is NOT accepted for** | **Every one of its counts — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §4 (D129-R2).** Rejected and **not owner-certified**: D128's accession counts as semantically complete; `counts_are_trustworthy`; `parser_state` as successful; **D128 as an E0-readiness proof**; and **any downstream research count based on the omitted shard population**. **No E0 authorization follows from D128.** A completed run is not a certified run — the run produced a full set of numbers, and they are wrong in a structured way no completion signal reveals. |
| **What went wrong in the D128 parse, and how much it cost** | **Bulk historical shards were dispatched to the wrong parser — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §5 (D129-R3), classified `PARSER_IMPLEMENTATION_DEFECT`.** `5,337` legitimate members matching the tracked `HISTORICAL_FILE_NAME_PATTERN` `^CIK[0-9]{10}-submissions-[0-9]{3}\.json$` were routed through `parse_submissions_document(...)` instead of the historical-submission contract, and were rejected — correctly, because that parser's contract is *one document describes one CIK* and a shard is not that document. **The defect is the dispatch, not the rejection.** Measured: **`5,102,087`** shard accessions, **`2,064,473`** recovered elsewhere, **`3,037,614` genuinely absent** from a real universe of **`19,034,205`** — an omitted share of **`15.96%`**, about **one accession in six**. |
| **Why the D128 omission is a research problem and not just a data-loss problem** | **Because it lands almost entirely on one arm of the temporal comparison — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §5 (D129-R3).** Form 10-K family: **`42,363`** accessions absent = **`13.57%`** across all years, but **`9.11%`** in the development cohort `2010`–`2021` against **`0.25%`** in the evaluation cohort `2022`–`2026` — roughly a **`36x`** differential. **This is recorded as a STRUCTURAL CONFOUND on the preregistered temporal comparison, not a negligible random loss**: it would bias exactly the quantity the study measures, in a direction set by an implementation defect. The frozen cohort windows are unchanged, and **no §25 deviation is recorded** — the counts are rejected before any preregistered analysis consumed them. |
| **What happened to registrant identity on the historical references** | **`4,144` distinct registrants collapsed to one wrong CIK — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §6 (D129-R4), classified `PARSER_IMPLEMENTATION_DEFECT`.** All **`5,337` of `5,337`** `census_historical_references` rows received **one incorrect observation-wide registrant CIK**. **The compounding hazard is the guard**: `_historical_registrant_cik(...)` in `src/disclosure_drift/m3/offline_parse.py` refuses unless the match is unique — `len(rows) != 1` — so zero candidates fail closed and two or more fail closed, but **one consistently wrong candidate passes silently and is then returned as authority**. A fail-closed check that only tests uniqueness certifies a single wrong answer as confidently as a single right one. |
| **What binds a historical shard to its registrant** | **The parent's explicit `filings.files[].name` declaration, never the filename — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §7 (D129-R5).** The CIK embedded in a shard filename **may be used only as corroborating consistency evidence** and **must never become the sole registrant-identity inference rule**: a name is a label, the parent's declaration is the assertion, and where they disagree no identity has been established. **Fail-closed behaviour is required for all four of**: no parent; multiple parents; conflicting parent identities; and a parent/member-name CIK contradiction wherever that comparison is performed. |
| **Whether archive member order may affect parse correctness** | **No — never — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §7 (D129-R6).** A shard may appear in the archive **before** its parent, and a corrected implementation must handle that through a **bounded, deterministic** mechanism — for example deferring shard member names and resolving them against an explicit parent mapping once the parents are known. **The invariants are frozen; the algorithm is not** — Decision 129 deliberately freezes no implementation beyond order-independence, boundedness, and determinism. |
| **Whether the new SEC fields in the source are a problem** | **No — they are accepted optional non-semantic fields — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §8 (D129-R7).** `lei`, `filings.recent.core_type`, and `filings.recent.isXBRLNumeric` are legitimate optional SEC fields and **may later be registered as known non-semantic fields**. **No code change is made**, and nothing changes about accession identity, registrant identity, the cohort definition, or study methodology. Recorded so ordinary upstream field growth is not mistaken for a defect — nor this acknowledgement for a schema change. |
| **Whether the D128 watchdog actually stopped anything** | **No — it could not have, and the run never received a signal — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §9 (D129-R10), forensic result `WATCHDOG_FALSE_ALERT_SIGNAL_NOT_DELIVERED_TO_CANARY`.** Watchdog v1 targeted Python PID `6456`; its socket condition was **false** because the `lsof` selectors were **ORed without `-a`**; the chain launched as a **background job from non-interactive `zsh`**, so Python **inherited `SIGINT = SIG_IGN`**; `kill -INT` therefore **had no effect**. **No rollback and no replay occurred**, compact evidence proves **one row per member**, and `/usr/bin/time` recorded **zero received signals**. **Before any corrected long run, `SIGINT` delivery must be positively proven**, and a future watchdog **may not treat a successful `kill(2)` return as proof that the process stopped** — `kill(2)` reports a signal was sent, which is a different fact. |
| **Why the D128 monitor reported stalls that were not stalls** | **Because the progress counter had correctly finished while the run had not — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §10 (D129-R11).** The `13` post-traversal stall alerts were **false**: member-count progress was complete while F1 and F2 **legitimately continued**, so a detector watching `compact_source_members` saw a counter at its terminal value and read it as a stall. Future member-count stall detection applies **only while `member_count < governed_member_count`**, or must use a phase-appropriate progress signal. **No code change now.** |
| **What a complete first source actually costs, now that one has run** | **`33 h 18 m 43 s` and a `96.5729 GiB` catalog — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §11.** Wall **`119,923.08` s**; parse ≈ `26 h 59 m 30 s`; F1 ≈ `1 h 44 m 47 s`; F2 ≈ `4 h 20 m 54 s`; final close and evidence ≈ `7 m 27 s`; post-parse total ≈ `6 h 19 m 12 s`. Peak RSS `3,178,020,864` bytes = **`2.9598 GiB`**; final working catalog `103,694,548,992` bytes = **`96.5729 GiB`**; minimum free `18,851,307,520` bytes = `17.5568 GiB`, so **the continuous `10 GiB` floor was never breached**; final free `28,195,401,728` bytes = `26.2589 GiB`. **One arithmetic condition is published rather than smoothed**: the three named post-parse components sum to `6 h 13 m 08 s`, leaving a **`364` s residual** attributed to no named component. **These are the costs of a DEFECTIVE run** — a corrected one stores more. |
| **How much room the pre-F2 gate actually had** | **About `2 GiB`, not about `7` — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §11.3, correcting the earlier summary.** Free space at admission was **≈ `31.98`–`32.01 GiB`**, **not the ≈ `37 GiB` first reported**, against the frozen floor `PRE_F2_MINIMUM_FREE_BYTES` = `32,212,254,720` bytes. **The gate admitted, and it did its job with far less room than anyone believed at the time.** The second correction is attribution: the **≈ `15.17 GiB` peak write-ahead log occurred BEFORE the gate**, during parse-finalization/resolution, while **F2's own WAL peak was ≈ `8.67 GiB`** — putting the run's largest transient demand in the phase the gate protects would make the gate look better calibrated than it is. |
| **Whether the D124 capacity model is enough to authorize the corrected rerun** | **No — a new reconciliation is required first — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §12 (D129-R12).** Three independent reasons: D128's actual storage and runtime **exceeded important D124 projections** — actual F1+F2 `6 h 05 m 41 s` against the [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) §8 planning ≈ `3.37 h`, an underprediction of **≈ `1.8x`–`1.9x`**; the real pre-F2 margin was only **≈ `2 GiB`**; and **a corrected parse adds millions of presently absent accessions and records**, so D128's `96.57 GiB` catalog is a **floor** rather than an estimate. **Decision 129 does not construct the new model**, and **no D124-R5 gate is relaxed by this** — the ruling is about the model's sufficiency as an authorization basis, not about any gate. |
| **Whether D128 can be resumed, repaired, or reused** | **No to all three — it must be rerun from scratch — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §14 (D129-R8).** A corrected complete-source proof requires **all four** of: **new code**, **a new run identity**, **a new create-once world**, and **a full source rerun from the beginning**. **D128 is nevertheless valuable pre-correction evidence** — the only complete-source observation that exists — so it is **RETAINED UNCHANGED**. Archival to the external `SSK SSD` and the internal reclamation that follows it are **D130**, and Decision 129 **archives, deletes, moves, and mutates nothing**. |
| **Whether the earlier canaries are invalidated by the D128 defect** | **No — none of them — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §13 (D129-R9).** D117, D120, D122, and D123 retain their prior acceptance for their stated purposes. They were performance, feasibility, and prefix experiments and **structurally could not have reached terminal parser semantic adjudication**: none traversed the complete source, so none could have met the shard population at the scale that made the defect visible. **Finding a defect only a complete traversal can expose is not a retroactive indictment of experiments that were never in a position to expose it.** |
| **What has to happen before another complete-source run** | **Five things, in order, each needing its own owner instrument — [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §16.** **(1)** D130, the D128 external archival and internal reclamation. **(2)** A bounded parser, reference, and watchdog correction stage. **(3)** Semantic validation. **(4)** The corrected-run capacity reconciliation (D129-R12). **(5)** The corrected complete-source canary. **Decision 129 implements no repair** — its §§7–10 invariants are specified and not written — and it **authorizes no execution**: **accepting a run's mechanics is not authorizing another run.** All three activation constants remain `None`, migration head remains `0015` with `0016` absent, and both network switches remain `false` at request ceiling `0`. **Complete source is NOT authorized. E0 is NOT authorized.** |
| **Whether the D128 world still exists internally** | **No — it was archived externally and deleted — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §8 (D130-R4).** All three internal paths — the world, the run log, and the empty `__sqlite_tmp` sibling — are **absent**. Deletion ran **only after** every archive, read-back, and source-recheck predicate passed, under explicit path guards and a **passing dry run**, with glob expansion disabled and `realpath` self-resolution required. **No generic cleanup occurred and none is authorized.** |
| **Where the D128 archive is, and how to recognise it** | **On the `SSK SSD`, and by the digests in [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §6 (D130-R2).** `/Volumes/SSK SSD/FDD_M3_3_D130_D128_ARCHIVE/d128_complete_first_source_v1.pax.tar`, `103,966,696,960` bytes, SHA-256 `b4f13e9277b4d6bc79462a2c44c599a48a8844173f18cefc829d255e42583013`; both manifests `3,645` bytes at `af5088e4ac1c387675d50ba933e187c20f95e0e4cb471bf157665f00e366fac4`. The volume is `/dev/disk4s2`, ExFAT, volume UUID `397A4D4A-9508-391E-814E-3B533C7BD049`. |
| **Where the durable index to that archive lives** | **In the decision record itself, and nowhere else — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §6 (D130-R2).** Unlike [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md), **no separate internal retention artifact was written for D130** — the manifests exist only on the removable volume. §6 therefore carries every identity needed to recognise, locate, or challenge the archive **if the volume is unavailable**. Do not create another standalone retention artifact unless convention requires one. |
| **Whether the archived D128 world can be used for the corrected run** | **No — it is cold evidence only — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §4.** Extracting the tar does **not** produce a world that may be resumed, continued, or promoted. [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §14 (D129-R8)'s four requirements — new code, a new run identity, a new create-once world, a full rerun from the beginning — are **unsoftened by archival**, and `D128_SEMANTIC_REPAIR_REQUIRED` is unchanged. |
| **Why the first D130 attempt stopped, and whether that was correct** | **Because no external device was attached at all, and yes — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §3 (D130-R1).** `diskutil list external physical` was empty, both Thunderbolt/USB4 ports reported `No device connected`, and `/Volumes/` held only `Macintosh HD`. **Nothing was archived, nothing staged, nothing deleted**, and the D128 set was re-verified intact. Owner-accepted under `M3_3_D130_STOP_NO_EXTERNAL_VOLUME_OWNER_ACCEPTED`. |
| **How the archive was verified, and why tar exiting `0` was not enough** | **By reading it back off the device with `F_NOCACHE` — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §7 (D130-R3).** `26` of `26` regular members re-hashed from the tar, `0` byte mismatches, `0` SHA mismatches, `0` missing, `0` unexpected, `0` absolute or traversal names. The source and tar-member manifests are **byte-identical**, which is stronger than the tuple-set equality the instrument allowed as a fallback. **Successful creation was never treated as verification.** |
| **What proves the archive survived the deletion** | **A full re-read taken after the internal copy was gone — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §7.** All `103,966,696,960` bytes were re-hashed with `F_NOCACHE` **after** deletion and returned the identical digest. The archive was read in full **four** times in total, three of them uncached, and every read agreed. |
| **How much internal space came back** | **`96.853 GiB` — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §9 (D130-R5).** Internal free went from `25.113 GiB` to `121.966 GiB`; external free from `386.004 GiB` to `289.174 GiB`. Reclaimed allocated space **need not equal** logical archived bytes and here exceeds them by `28,663,426` bytes — APFS allocation, not extra deletion. |
| **Whether the reclaimed space authorizes another complete canary** | **No — free space is not run readiness — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §10 (D130-R6).** `121.966 GiB` clears [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md)'s historical `>= 105 GiB` threshold and **that is not an authorization**. [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §12 (D129-R12) controls: a **new corrected-run capacity reconciliation** comes first. **No D124-R5 gate is relaxed.** |
| **What survived D130, and what was deliberately left alone** | **Every neighbour, and the idle `tmux` session — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §11.** Retained: `m3_3_perf_c3_local_40k_v1`, `~/m3-d117-diagnostics`, `~/m3-retention`, `~/m3-private-evidence`, and `FDD_M3_D125_ARCHIVE`. The now-empty `~/m3-run-logs` directory remains. The idle `tmux` session named `d128` is **not D128 evidence**, held no handle in any D128 path, and was **not touched** — ordinary operator housekeeping, no action from this record. |
| **What happens after D130** | **D131 repair first, and four steps after it — [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §12 (D130-R7).** D131 must address **separately and explicitly**: correct bulk historical-shard dispatch; per-reference explicit parent registrant binding; the recognized optional fields `lei`, `filings.recent.core_type`, `filings.recent.isXBRLNumeric`; **proven** `SIGINT` delivery for watchdog stop actions; and corrected post-traversal stall monitoring. **Then** bounded semantic validation, **then** bounded performance A/B optimization, **then** the corrected-run capacity reconciliation, **then** an owner decision on a corrected canary. **E0 remains unauthorized throughout.** |
| **Whether the two D128 parser defects are repaired** | **Yes, in code, and the repair is published with the code — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §1.** D131 is the first record in this sequence whose implementation, tests, and governance record enter in **the same commit**, so the record and the artifact are verifiable against each other by inspection. **It repairs defects and certifies no count**: [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §4 (D129-R2)'s rejection of every D128 count **stands entirely and is not revisited**. The outcome token is `D128_SEMANTIC_DEFECTS_REPAIRED_IN_CODE_AND_VALIDATED_IN_REPOSITORY`. |
| **How the corrected traversal routes a historical shard** | **It defers the shard's name and reopens it after the parent map is complete — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §4.** A shard **never reaches** `parse_submissions_document(...)`. Meeting one records **only** its `member_ordinal` and `member_name` — never its bytes — and every deferred shard is parsed after the traversal ends, in **original governed archive ordinal order**, through the existing `parse_historical_submissions(...)`. **Archive ordering does not change semantic output**: parent-before-child and child-before-parent produce the same result, which is D129-R6 read literally. |
| **What binds a shard to its registrant in the corrected code** | **The explicit parent declaration, and nothing else — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §4, implementing D129-R5.** `filings.files[].name` is authoritative; the CIK in a shard's own filename is **corroboration only** — it may confirm a declaration and may refuse one that contradicts it, and it is never a binding. `_resolve_shard_parent(...)` **fails closed** on all three shapes: no declaring parent, more than one distinct declaring parent, and a filename CIK contradicting the declared parent. **Every parent is resolved before any member is reopened**, so a refusal costs no decompression and cannot half-parse the deferred population. |
| **How the corrected code keeps the shard phase bounded** | **By holding no payload and bounding the parent map by the shard population — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §4 (D131-R5).** The deferred record has **no payload field** by construction, and a parent declaration is retained **only** when the archive carries a member of exactly that name, so residency grows with shards rather than with members. A live per-boundary residency test fails on any per-member state introduced into the bulk generator. |
| **How registrant identity is now stamped on a historical reference** | **By the document that declared it, through a required field — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §5.** `HistoricalFileReference.registrant_cik_padded` is **required with no default**, which is the point: a construction site cannot omit it and silently inherit somebody else's registrant. Persistence writes that per-reference value, **no observation-wide CIK is reused anywhere**, and **valid and malformed** reference persistence both preserve parent identity. An unusable parent CIK **raises** rather than substituting a value. |
| **Whether the binding repair needed a migration** | **No — the schema already supported it — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §5.** `registrant_cik_padded` already exists on both reference tables and is already part of the primary key, so the corrected value lands in a column the schema had already contracted. **Migration `0016` is neither required nor authorized.** The former per-observation lookup that the D111 remediation introduced is **deleted rather than bypassed**, and its per-reference scan cost does not return. |
| **How the three new SEC fields are recognized without breaking anything** | **By recognizing them without giving them a shape contract — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §6 (D131-R1).** `lei`, `filings.recent.core_type`, and `filings.recent.isXBRLNumeric` join the **known** set. The two `filings.recent` fields go in `KNOWN_OPTIONAL_RECENT_FIELDS`, deliberately **not** in `ACCESSION_ARRAY_FIELDS`, because that registry carries a **list-shape contract**: a present scalar there is a blocking `malformed_nested_array` event that quarantines the whole block — **a new refusal invented by the act of recognizing a field**, strictly worse than the drift report it was meant to silence. Neither field becomes required, and **no accession identity, registrant identity, cohort, or preregistered methodology definition changes**. |
| **Which parser versions moved, and why both had to** | **`submissions-json` to `1.2` and `submissions-historical` to `1.1` — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §7 (D131-R2, D131-R8).** Both parsers' observable `unknown_fields` output changed, so the prior strings no longer describe the implementations. Both are **derived**: `PARSER_VERSIONS` imports each parser module's own constant and `SourceSpec.parser_version` reads it, so a second hand-maintained copy cannot fall behind. **No migration is required** — `unknown_fields_json` is a pre-existing `TEXT` column whose content semantics move while its DDL does not. |
| **What happens to provenance already recorded under the old parser versions** | **It stands as historical truth and is never rewritten — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §7.** A row recorded under `submissions-json/1.1` correctly describes the implementation that produced it, and **no `UPDATE` of any `parser_version` exists anywhere in `src/`**. What changes is **reuse**: `versions_agree(...)` is an exact match with `None` never agreeing, `require_parser_version(...)` refuses before provenance is written, and `evaluate_reuse(...)` fails on `parser_compatibility_known` so the refusal is attributable. **A stale version is refused in both directions** — being merely older than the last version anybody remembered is not a defence. |
| **Where the exact named-member archive read lives, and what it preserves** | **On the public `sec.archive` surface, as `iter_named_members(...)` — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §8 (D131-R3).** The deferred phase reopens shards through **one public surface**, so the defences applied on reopen are the same implementation the primary traversal applied rather than a second expression of the same answers elsewhere. It preserves archive refusal/type/size protections, **exact** requested-member identity, deterministic requested order, absent-and-ambiguous refusal, **one-member-at-a-time** residency, and **no network**. The archive-level *cumulative* expansion cap is deliberately not re-applied: those bytes were already admitted under it, and re-counting a subset from zero would compare part of an archive against a whole-archive limit. |
| **How the corrected watchdog proves a stop actually happened** | **By watching the target terminate, never by trusting `kill(2)` — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §9 (D131-R6).** The launch is **foreground** and `exec`-based and **refuses** an inherited `SIGINT = SIG_IGN` — the exact D128 condition. The stop authenticates the **exact PID and command line** (an empty `--expect-command` is refused outright, because `"" in observed` holds for every process), sends **`SIGINT` only**, verifies termination, and **never escalates to `SIGTERM` or `SIGKILL`** — a survivor is `STOP_FAILED` at exit `4`. `ProcessLookupError` is **already gone**; `PermissionError` is **not success**. The network probe uses the selector **intersection** `lsof -nP -a -p PID -i`. |
| **What the watchdog does with a `--pid` of `0` or `-1`** | **Refuses it before touching anything — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §9 (D131-R9).** `os.kill` reads `0` as the caller's own process group — from the canary's pane, the canary and the watchdog together — and `-1` as every process the user may signal; `lsof -p 0` and `lsof -p -1` are the same mistake in the reading direction. The domain is strictly **`pid > 0`**, written down **once** in `non_targetable_pid_detail(...)` and read by both PID-taking operations, and it is checked on the argument **before** any process inspection, **before** any `lsof` construction, and **before** any signalling. `STOP_REFUSED_NON_POSITIVE_PID` and `PROBE_REFUSED_NON_POSITIVE_PID` both **exit `3`**, send nothing, and run no `lsof`. |
| **What the monitor does when the member counts disagree** | **Gives it its own verdict and its own exit code — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §10 (D131-R10).** `observed < governed` is a real stall (exit `2`); `observed == governed` **disables** member-count alerting (exit `0`), which is the D128 false-alert case; and `observed > governed` is `MEMBER_COUNT_INCONSISTENT` at **exit `5`**. **Exit `5` is a claim about the monitor's own inputs**: it terminates only the monitor invocation, **sends no signal**, **does not stop the canary**, and is distinct from a stall alert, a refusal, and `STOP_FAILED`. **No F1/F2 automatic wall-clock kill rule was added.** |
| **Whether a bounded `--member-limit` run proves the shard repair** | **No — it parses ZERO shards — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §11 (D131-R7).** A prefix stops mid-archive, so its parent map is **incomplete by construction**, and resolving a shard against it would refuse a well-formed archive. A shard met inside the prefix counts against the bound as a member the traversal handled and is simply never parsed. **A run that parses zero shards cannot demonstrate that shards are parsed correctly**, and reading a clean prefix as evidence would repeat D128's error in the opposite direction. The bounded real semantic proof needs a **separately authorized semantic fixture or mode**. |
| **What still blocks any future network authorization** | **`CensusOrchestrator._parse_bulk`, deliberately left unrepaired — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §12 (D131-R4).** It is the twin of the path Repair A corrected and **still carries the same dispatch defect**. It is safe **today** only because it sits behind `require_network()`, network is disabled at both switches at request ceiling `0`, and neither the corrected offline canary nor E0 uses it — **unreachable is a property of the configuration, not of the code**. **OWNER RULE: no future network or live-retrieval authorization may reach that path until its historical-shard dispatch is repaired.** It is **not** a blocker to D131 acceptance and is **not** to be repaired now — repairing it would expand an accepted change set after its validation. |
| **Whether D131 changed anything about performance or capacity** | **Nothing at all — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §13.** No SQLite cache, batching, checkpoint-cadence, WAL-mode, `synchronous`, index-architecture, multiprocessing/writer, or D127 pre-F2 gate change. Current free space is **informational only** — an input to a capacity model, never a substitute for one — and [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §12 (D129-R12) still requires a **new corrected-run capacity reconciliation** that D131 does not construct. |
| **How D131 was validated, and what the final gate returned** | **Seven passes ending in one repository gate — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §14.** Implementation, fresh independent review, correction pass, fresh bounded rereview, two final corrections (`submissions-historical/1.1` and the `pid > 0` domain), a final independent delta recheck that mutation-authenticated both corrections and proved the WIP byte-identical across the review, and then `make check-fast` **exit `0`, run exactly once**: **`4898` passed, `1` skipped, `0` failed** of `4899` collected, with every static, config, and navigation gate green. The single skip is **pre-existing and unrelated** — `tests/unit/test_m23_pilot_manifest.py:429`. |
| **What happens after D131** | **Bounded real semantic proof first, and three steps after it — [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) §17.** (1) A **bounded real semantic proof** of the corrected shard dispatch and per-reference binding, which needs a separately authorized semantic fixture or mode because D131-R7 rules that an ordinary prefix parses zero shards; (2) **bounded performance A/B**; (3) the **corrected-run capacity reconciliation** (D129-R12); (4) **only then**, an owner decision on another complete-source canary. Each step requires its own owner instrument, and **E0 remains unauthorized throughout**. **The capacity reconciliation is not the immediate next step.** |
| **Whether the D131 repair was ever proven against real SEC bytes** | **Yes, over seven authenticated real members — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §1.** The proof is a **retrospective** record: it ran under the owner instrument `M3_3_D132_BOUNDED_REAL_SEMANTIC_PROOF_AUTHORIZED`, issued outside this repository and now spent, and the record authorized none of it. What is bounded is the **population**, not the authenticity — every payload the proof parsed came byte-identically out of the accepted bulk archive. Classification: `BOUNDED_REAL_SEMANTIC_FIXTURE_ONLY`. |
| **Whether proving the repair needed new code or a new mode** | **No — the accepted D131 path ran unchanged — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §3 (D132-R1).** The proof drove `select_planned_source` → `materialize_one_planned_source` → `_stream_bulk_submissions` → shard classification and deferral → explicit parent resolution → `sec.archive.iter_named_members` → `parse_historical_submissions` → `CensusCatalog.persist_streamed` → reverse lookup. **No repository semantics were reimplemented**; the scratch harness supplied disposable fixture provenance and world initialization only, which is what makes the result a measurement of the shipped implementation rather than of a parallel one. |
| **Which source the bounded proof read, and whether it is the D128 population** | **The accepted bulk archive, and yes — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §4 (D132-R2).** `sec_bulk_submissions-c85744be921b0dc5.zip`, `1,556,847,020` B, SHA-256 `c85744be…82b82f`, `985,834` governed JSON members, resolved governed through `census_plan_sources` → `census_source_observations` → `SnapshotStore.payload_path`. Central-directory corroboration returns `5,337` historical shards across `4,144` distinct parent CIKs — **exactly the figures [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §§5–6 recorded for the two defects**. Read-only; SHA and mtime unchanged afterwards. |
| **How the four proof shards were chosen** | **By a stated rule over the real central directory — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §5 (D132-R3).** Lowest shard-parent CIK with at least two shards (first two), then the two lowest with exactly one: `0000001750`/`1,354`, `0000001800`/`2,001` and `608`, `0000002034`/`172`. All four physically exist, are **explicitly declared by their real parent's `filings.files[].name`**, match the governed shard shape, and carry real historical accessions. Selection cost **`7` payload reads against a `24` budget** — a selection, not a survey. |
| **Whether the child-before-parent case was invented for the test** | **No — the real archive supplied it — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §5.** `CIK0000001800-submissions-002.json` occurs **before** its parent document in the accepted source, so the ordering hazard [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §7 (D129-R6) names is a property of the real data rather than a fixture contrivance. |
| **What the proof fixtures actually contained** | **The same seven byte-exact real SEC payloads in two different orders — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §6 (D132-R4).** No JSON normalization, no reserialization, no field edits, no CIK edits, no synthesized accessions. `PARENT_FIRST` `d99c264a…95ea8` and `CHILD_FIRST` `efcf4e20…4477d`, **each `169,186` B**, with member name, payload length, and payload SHA-256 equality proven across both and every payload matching its source member. The archives hash differently only because the members are laid out differently — the single variable is order. |
| **Where each member was actually dispatched** | **Every shard to the historical parser, in both orders — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §7 (D132-R5).** `3` primaries to `parse_submissions_document` at `submissions-json/1.2`; `4` shards to `parse_historical_submissions` at `submissions-historical/1.1`; **`0`** shards reaching the primary parser and **`4` of `4`** reaching the historical one — the direct contradiction of D128, where all `5,337` reached the primary parser and were rejected. Instrumentation was **transparent call-through only**, and two **non-instrumented** worlds corroborated the same result through durable `record_path` provenance. |
| **Whether registrant identity survived on real data** | **Yes, across three distinct registrants — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §8 (D132-R6).** For every shard the primary's `filings.files[].name` declaration, the persisted `census_historical_references.registrant_cik_padded`, the reverse lookup, and the parser's `registrant_cik` argument **all agreed**: `1750 → 1750`, `1800 → 1800`, `1800 → 1800`, `2034 → 2034`. **No observation-wide CIK leaked** — the collapse [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §6 found did not occur. D129-R5 stays controlling: the declaration is authoritative, the filename corroborative only. |
| **How much real historical material the repair restored** | **`4,135` accessions — for four shards, and for nothing more — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §9 (D132-R7).** `1,354 + 2,001 + 608 + 172`, and **all `4,135` are absent from the corresponding selected primary documents**, which is precisely the material D128 lost. **THIS IS NOT A SOURCE-WIDE COUNT.** It measures four shards belonging to three registrants, selected from `5,337` shards belonging to `4,144`, and must not be extrapolated to the archive. |
| **Whether archive order changed the semantic result** | **No — the comparison returned `PASS` — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §10 (D132-R8).** Both orders produced `7` members, `7,139` records, `7,139` parsed, `0` quarantined, `0` malformed references, `parser_state` `completed`, with **no differences** across accession identity, canonical record content, applicable record SHA, registrant binding, historical-reference rows, parser provenance, reverse lookup, quarantine, malformed references, member representation, or applicable F1/F2 material. This is D129-R6 measured rather than argued. |
| **Whether the matching completeness digests prove order-independent provenance** | **No — that equality is incidental and is recorded as such — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §10.** The completeness digest, parser run id, and member-manifest ordinals happened to agree because **D131 defers every shard to the end of the traversal**, so both orderings converge on the same three-primaries-then-four-shards sequence. **It must NOT be generalized to arbitrary archive permutations** — it follows from the deferral behaviour over this member set and is not an invariant this proof established. |
| **Whether a shard's filename can stand in for a missing parent declaration** | **No — the implementation refuses — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §11 (D132-R9).** A fixture holding the four byte-exact real shards with their **declaring primaries omitted**, filenames still encoding the correct CIKs, was refused under the D129-R5 missing-parent rule: `0` parsed records, `0` accessions, `0` references, `0` parser runs, plan `parser_state` `not_started`. The refusal cost no partial state. **Contradicted-parent behaviour was NOT reproduced** — that would have required modifying real JSON bytes — and the **D131 negative unit proof remains controlling for contradiction**. |
| **Where the D132 evidence lives, and why it is not in the repository** | **In a private disposable root, pointed to by the record — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §12 (D132-R10).** The manifest `~/m3-d132-semantic-proof/d132_proof_manifest.json`, SHA-256 `732e696a…95b3a7`, authenticates the run; the `~96 MB` root is **not copied into git** and no part of it is tracked. **This Decision record is the durable governance pointer**, the same architecture [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §6 (D130-R2) adopted, and four scratch-harness SHA-256s authenticate the instrument compactly. |
| **What the proof mutated** | **Nothing — and the non-mutation was measured — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §13 (D132-R11).** Repository unchanged (`0` files touched); operational catalog bytes unchanged at SHA-256 `57e36a78…af2ded`, **matching two prior independent evidence artifacts** rather than only itself; accepted source SHA **and** mtime unchanged; network prohibited by configuration **and** additionally guarded by raising `socket` stubs. No E0, no E0 namespace, no canary, no `census_orchestrator` import, no migration `0016`, no authority change. |
| **What D132 does and does not certify** | **Bounded real semantics only — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §14 (D132-R12).** It certifies the behaviour of the D131 repair over the authenticated seven-member fixture. It certifies **no** source-wide completeness, accession count, or record count; **no** corrected D128 counts; **no** replacement of D128; and **no** E0, canary, performance, or capacity readiness. **[Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §4 (D129-R2) remains fully controlling — every D128 count remains rejected** — and **§12 (D129-R12) remains unresolved.** `7` members of `985,834` were parsed. |
| **What happens after D132** | **Bounded performance A/B first, and two steps after it — [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §17 (D132-R13).** (1) A **bounded performance A/B**; (2) the **corrected-run capacity reconciliation** under [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §12 (D129-R12), using the repaired parser and the adopted performance configuration; (3) **only then** an owner decision on another complete-source canary, which §14 (D129-R8) still requires to be a full rerun from scratch in a new world. Each step needs its own owner instrument, **E0 remains unauthorized throughout**, and `CensusOrchestrator._parse_bulk` remains an open PRE-NETWORK blocker (D131-R4). |
| **Why the accepted D131 watchdog could not stop a canary on Linux** | **`ps` truncated the command line at `80` columns — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §3 (D133-R1).** `/bin/ps -o command= -p PID` on Ubuntu/`procps` truncates to `80` columns when its stdout is not a terminal, which is exactly what `capture_output` gives it, and a canary's command line is a virtualenv interpreter followed by an absolute path. The expected substring fell off the end, so the watchdog **correctly failed safe** with `STOP_REFUSED_TARGET_MISMATCH` and **never signalled a legitimate target**. It is a `PRODUCT_DEFECT` in operator tooling, **not** a semantic-pipeline defect. |
| **Why a green macOS `make check-fast` did not catch it** | **Because macOS BSD `ps` does not truncate in that condition — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §3.** The local gate was run and was green, and it was green **truthfully about macOS**. This is a **platform-conditional escape**, not a gap in the D131 validation discipline: no end-to-end test written on the developer platform could have failed, which is also why the guard against recurrence had to be written at a different level (§5). |
| **What the repair actually was, and what it left alone** | **One option, `-ww`, and everything else untouched — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §4 (D133-R2).** `/bin/ps -ww -o command= -p PID` restores unlimited-width observation on **both** BSD `ps` and `procps`, so one argv serves both platforms with **no platform branch and no `/proc` fallback**.
