Unchanged: the positive-PID domain, the `-o state=` probe, exact-target authentication semantics, `SIGINT`-only policy, `STOP_FAILED`, `ProcessLookupError`, `PermissionError`, the `lsof -nP -a -p PID -i` intersection, and `MEMBER_COUNT_INCONSISTENT` at exit `5`. The published change set is `2` files, `+49 / -2`. |
| **Why the regression test asserts an argv instead of a behaviour** | **Because the behaviour it protects is invisible on macOS — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §5 (D133-R3).** The four originally Linux-failing tests **still pass on macOS without `-ww`**, so a behavioural assertion cannot protect the repair on the developer platform; only the *request* distinguishes the two implementations. `test_the_command_probe_asks_ps_for_unlimited_width` asserts the actual `ps` argument vector, is platform-independent, and **removing `-ww` is killed by that exact test**. |
| **What proves the repair works on Linux** | **A fresh push-triggered Ubuntu CI run over the repaired tree — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §7 (D133-R5).** Workflow `CI`, run `32605572777`, SHA `977a811b…`, **`SUCCESS`**; the SEC-enabled required job and the core-environment job both `SUCCESS`; Linux pytest `4899` passed, `1` skipped, `0` failed. **All four previously failing nodes passed** under `ubuntu-latest`/`procps`, the new regression test passed, and the previously skipped CLI smoke, secret scan, and repository hygiene steps **executed and passed**. **No manual workflow rerun was used**. |
| **Why Decision 133 was published after its code instead of with it** | **Because the claim is about Linux and the developer platform is macOS — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §1.** The code shipped at `977a811b…` deliberately **without** a governance record: a record written from macOS alone could have asserted the repair but could not have established it. The delay is a **methodological requirement**, not a convenience, and it is a different shape from [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md), which entered with its code in one commit. |
| **Whether D131's watchdog rulings are still accepted** | **Yes, and now on both platforms — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §8 (D133-R6).** D131's **semantic** acceptance was **never invalidated**; its **operational** half was temporarily reopened when Linux CI showed the exact-target authentication rule was not satisfied there. With the local validation and the green Ubuntu CI, **D131-R6 and D131-R9 are again accepted cross-platform** for the currently governed environments. Their **content is unchanged** — only their platform coverage was ever in question. |
| **Whether the Linux defect touched the D132 semantic proof** | **No — the two are disjoint — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §9 (D133-R7).** [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) remains **fully accepted and unchanged**. The defect and its repair are operator-tooling only and touch none of shard dispatch, parent binding, parser provenance, the restored historical accessions, or archive-order semantic equivalence. **No D132 claim is reopened**, and the watchdog was not an input to that proof. |
| **Whether D131 or D132 should have been reverted** | **No, and neither was — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §10 (D133-R8).** The failure was **fail-safe**: a legitimate target was **refused**, and an unintended target was **not signalled**. No persisted project data was corrupted and **no semantic result changed**. Reverting would have pulled the accepted D131 repairs off `main` in exchange for a defect that already fails closed — trading a two-file correction for a wide one. **The minimal repair was the correct disposition.** |
| **What D133 changes about the blockers and the sequence** | **Nothing — [Decision 133](Decisions/decision_133_m3_3_watchdog_linux_portability_repair.md) §§11, 14 (D133-R9, D133-R10).** `census_orchestrator.py::_parse_bulk` remains an open **PRE-NETWORK** blocker; [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §12 (D129-R12)'s corrected-run capacity reconciliation **remains unresolved**; §4 (D129-R2)'s rejection of every D128 semantic count **remains controlling**. The [Decision 132](Decisions/decision_132_m3_3_bounded_real_semantic_proof.md) §17 sequence — bounded performance A/B, then capacity reconciliation, then an owner decision on another complete-source canary — is **neither advanced nor reordered**. **None of the blockers blocks the next bounded performance A/B**, and each step still requires its own owner instrument. |
| **Whether any performance change was adopted after the bounded A/B** | **No — none — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §8 (D134-R1).** The formal outcome is `D134_NO_LOW_RISK_PERFORMANCE_CHANGE_WORTH_ADOPTING`. `PRAGMA mmap_size` and relaxed checkpoint cadence were **both measured and both rejected**; **neither is implemented**; the accepted [Decision 131](Decisions/decision_131_m3_3_d128_semantic_and_operational_repair.md) runtime configuration and the accepted [Decision 119](Decisions/decision_119_m3_3_cache_bound_persistence_and_prefix_diagnostic.md) pragma surface are **unchanged**. The record's executable change set is **empty** — no source, test, script, configuration, schema, or migration byte moved for it. |
| **Why `PRAGMA mmap_size` was rejected despite measuring faster** | **Because `12.84%` and `9.775%` are below the `>= 15%` threshold and the benefit was trending DOWN — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §4 (D134-R2).** SQLite `3.53.4` clamped the `4,294,967,296`-byte request to **`2,147,418,112`** (`MAX_MMAP_SIZE=0x7fff0000`); the baseline read back `0`, so the arms were **non-vacuous**. Map coverage fell `80.64%` → `45.33%` between the two operating points, so **the advantage declines as the catalog grows — the wrong direction for complete source**. Peak RSS rose `+1,795,063,808` and `+1,427,111,936` bytes against Decision 120's `2.271` GiB baseline peak on an `8` GiB host, an I/O error on a mapped page arrives as **`SIGBUS`**, and adoption would have required changing the accepted D119 pragma-surface test. |
| **Why relaxing the checkpoint cadence bought nothing** | **Because F2, not F0/F1 cadence, sets peak WAL — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §5 (D134-R3).** The reduction was **non-vacuous and large**: `5,120` eligible boundaries, `5,120` → **`160`** checkpoints issued, `4,960` suppressed, a **`32×`** cadence reduction. It returned **`1.185%`**, `IMMATERIAL_<5`, and **WAL high-water was byte-identical at `768,054,552` bytes**. Peak WAL is set entirely by **F2's single association transaction, which neither arm checkpoints inside**, so the F0/F1 cadence never governed the peak. **Any future proposal to control peak WAL by tuning F0/F1 cadence is answered here in advance: it cannot work.** |
| **Why the `ab6000` arms ran only once** | **A precommitted early-stop rule fired — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §6 (D134-R4).** The amendment was issued **before** the `ab6000` arms ran. The mmap repetition returned `9.775%`, landing in the precommitted `>= 5%`, `< 10%` case whose action is `STOP_AFTER_MMAP_R1`. Counts matched the baseline exactly, the arm was non-vacuous, and no measurement anomaly was flagged. **Reduced repetition is an accepted consequence of a rule fixed in advance, not a defect requiring more execution**, and no cancelled arm is restarted. |
| **Whether the candidates changed the census** | **No — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §7 (D134-R5).** Across `8` measured arms, all `6` arm-to-baseline comparisons were semantically equal: **`0` stable-column table differences** across `27` tables per arm, compact-evidence member digest equal, semantic result document equal with `0` field differences. `10` tables differ **only** in already-classified volatile columns — the expected signature of two runs producing the same census. It establishes that **the candidates did not perturb semantics on the measured fixtures**, and **nothing** population- or source-wide. |
| **Why the first D134 cleanup attempt deleted nothing** | **Because the audit evidence existed only inside the deletion targets — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §9 (D134-R8).** Every fixture hash, per-arm pragma read-back, per-table digest, compact-evidence digest, and semantic-result digest lived **only** in the disposable `worlds/` and `fixtures/` trees, with no copy in the retained evidence set or anywhere else. The executing session **stopped without deleting anything**. Had it proceeded, §7's equivalence claim would have become **permanently unverifiable** — a reader could see the verdict `true` and never re-check it. |
| **What survives the D134 reclamation, and whether it is enough** | **`32` byte-exact JSON artifacts and a manifest — and yes, proved rather than asserted — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §9 (D134-R8).** `2,919,526` bytes were promoted byte-exactly (`32/32` verified by `cmp` **and** an independent `shasum` pass; `0` missing, `0` mismatch, `0` collision) and authenticated by `evidence/d134_preservation_manifest.json` (`39,960` bytes, SHA-256 `68ed5537af2be388b7ebdf7bff6545f71be3c586649104bb3523731aee6d60b3`). An eleven-row audit matrix passed **before** deletion, and **the published `12.84%` / `9.775%` / `1.185%` and all `6` semantic verdicts were RE-DERIVED from the preserved copies alone**. Only then were both trees deleted, reclaiming **`23.891` GiB**. |
| **Where a future auditor reads the D134 arm documents** | **`evidence/preserved_world_json/<run_id>/` — not `worlds/` — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §9.** The retained `harness/d134_compare.py` still resolves arm documents at `worlds/<run_id>/d134_semantics.json`, a path that **no longer exists**, so re-running that comparator requires repointing its root. **No data is missing** — the re-derivations above were performed against the preserved copies — but the path in the harness is **stale by construction**. |
| **What D134 changes about the blockers and the sequence** | **It closes the performance step and nothing else — [Decision 134](Decisions/decision_134_m3_3_bounded_performance_ab.md) §§11, 14 (D134-R6, D134-R7).** [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §12 (**D129-R12**) remains **unresolved and is now the NEXT SUBSTANTIVE STAGE**, requiring its own owner instrument; free space is **informational only**, an input to a capacity model and never a substitute for one. `CensusOrchestrator._parse_bulk` remains an **open PRE-NETWORK blocker** (D131-R4) — unreachable is a property of the current configuration, not of the code. **A complete-source canary is NOT authorized and E0 is NOT authorized.** |
| **Whether the corrected-run capacity reconciliation D129-R12 demanded now exists** | **Yes — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §1.** [Decision 129](Decisions/decision_129_m3_3_d128_semantic_adjudication.md) §12 (**D129-R12**) ruled the old [Decision 124](Decisions/decision_124_m3_3_capacity_reconciliation.md) model insufficient as an authorization basis and stated plainly that D129 **does not construct** the replacement. D135 constructs it. **But answering D129-R12 as a modelling obligation does NOT discharge it as a gate** — the model's own verdict is `D135_CORRECTED_RUN_CAPACITY_MODEL_INSUFFICIENT`, and *insufficient* attaches to the **capacity**, not the model: *the model is defensible; the machine does not currently hold what it asks for*. |
| **What free space a corrected complete-source run would need at launch** | **`185` GiB / `198,642,237,440` bytes — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §7 (D135-R2).** Remaining projected peak growth `158,648,106,397` B (`147.7526` GiB), safety reserve `39,662,026,600` B where the **`25%` branch binds**, required free `198,310,132,997` B (`184.6907` GiB), rounded up to the next `5` GiB. **This supersedes the `105` GiB launch gate for planning purposes**, which was built against D124 projections that D129 §11.4 measured as underpredicting F1+F2 by about `1.8×`–`1.9×`. |
| **Why the `30` GiB pre-F2 gate is not adequate, and what replaces it** | **`50` GiB / `53,687,091,200` bytes — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §8 (D135-R3).** The corrected projection consumes **`27.5539` GiB of the old `30` GiB gate outright, leaving `2.4461` GiB and ZERO safety reserve** — almost exactly the `~2 GiB` margin D129 §11.3 found D128 *actually* experienced. **The old gate is not a comfortable threshold that happens to be low; it is a near-miss restated at a larger workload.** The corrected floor carries remaining growth `29,585,782,350` B plus a **`20` GiB reserve floor** (the `25%` branch yields only `7,396,445,588` B). |
| **Whether the `30` GiB constant in code was changed** | **No — and changing it is NOT authorized — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §8 and §17.** `PRE_F2_MINIMUM_FREE_BYTES` in `src/disclosure_drift/m3/single_source_canary.py` **remains `30 * 1024**3`**. D135's executable change set is **empty**. Implementing the corrected constant is a separate, separately authorized change, and deferring it is safe precisely because **no run is authorized**: a gate that is never reached cannot be reached at the wrong value. |
| **Which capacity model governs, and why the D134-derived one never does** | **Model A governs every component — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §§4–6.** Model A scales D128's measured live state by the governing uplift **`U_rows` = `21,098,678/15,996,591` ≈ `1.31895`** — the conservative supported proxy, since `census_accessions`' `accession_plain` PRIMARY KEY collapses duplicates while `census_parsed_records` and `census_accession_observations` carry **their own surrogate keys**, so duplicate shard accessions **do** add rows. **Model B — D134 baseline arms only — is demonstrably an UNDERSTATEMENT for every component it covers**: it has **no pre-F2 WAL term at all** (D134's global WAL high-water *is* F2's, so D128's `~15.17 GiB` pre-F2 phenomenon is **unrepresented, not absent**), no pre-F2 breakpoint, and a low-ordinal prefix carrying `~317` accessions/member against a corrected source-wide `~19.3`. **Its density RISES with scale** (`+4.06%` between the two points), so extrapolating from it is an understatement **rather than a conservative bound**. |
| **What makes the capacity model auditable rather than asserted** | **Classified inputs, two independent arithmetic paths, and an internally closing timeline — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §§3, 6 (D135-R1).** Every durable input is a **MEASURED** value from an accepted record or from byte-verified D134 preserved evidence; every component is classified `MEASURED_LIVE` / `DERIVED` / `ARCHIVE_ONLY` / `UNKNOWN`, and **no `ARCHIVE_ONLY` figure is used as a live one**. Every derived value is reproduced by **two independently written exact-integer implementations** — Python `Fraction` + `ceil` and BSD `bc` at `scale=0` — agreeing **exactly on all `21` integer-byte totals**. And the D128 reconstruction **closes internally**: drawdown `15,486,956,011` B minus recovery `9,344,094,208` B gives F2 durable growth `6,142,861,803` B, matching F2's published `~8.67 GiB` WAL peak to within **`34,752,593` B**. |
| **Why the free-space measurement can be trusted not to have been fitted** | **The model was locked and hashed BEFORE it — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §9.** `capacity_model_locked.json`, SHA-256 `cba5ba2b…b69a982`, carries the attestation `WRITTEN BEFORE ANY CURRENT FREE-SPACE OBSERVATION`, and the observation document records **that same hash** as the model it was compared against. **No threshold was altered in response to the measurement.** Ordering is the guarantee: a floor chosen after seeing the disk is a floor chosen to be met. |
| **Whether the internal volume can host a corrected run** | **No — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §9 (D135-R4).** Minimum observed free **`126,846,775,296` B (`118.1353` GiB)** against a `185` GiB floor — **shortfall `71,795,462,144` B / `66.8647` GiB** — with **`0` APFS and `0` Time Machine snapshots** withholding space, so the shortfall is real. Three structural findings: the floor is **`81.04%` of the entire `228.2739` GiB container**; **the projected FINAL durable state alone (`127.7441` GiB) already EXCEEDS current free space by `9.6089` GiB** before any transient, WAL, or reserve — *the finished catalog does not fit, never mind the run that builds it*; and current free space **does** clear the obsolete `105` GiB gate by `13.1353` GiB, **precisely the trap [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §10 (D130-R6) named — clearing a superseded gate is not readiness**. |
| **Whether an internal cleanup campaign was ordered** | **No — it is NOT required and NOT authorized — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §10 (D135-R4, D135-R5).** Finding 2 above is why: the *final* state alone already exceeds current free space, so no plausible cleanup closes a `66.8647` GiB gap against a container where the target floor is `81.04%` of total capacity. **The measurement was descriptive only** — nothing was cleaned, deleted, moved, or reclaimed to produce it. |
| **What the next capacity-architecture step is** | **Bounded qualification of an ACTIVE EXTERNAL WORKING VOLUME — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §10 (D135-R5).** The owner selects the external-volume path because internal storage cannot practically be freed. It **reverses no prior implementation automatically** and authorizes **only a subsequent D136 qualification instrument**. It does **not** authorize using any SSD as the production working root, modifying path configuration, or running the complete-source canary. **Selecting a path is not qualifying a volume, and qualifying a volume is not adopting it** — three distinct steps, each needing its own instrument. |
| **What a candidate external volume must prove before it can be adopted** | **Six independent requirements — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §10 (D135-R5).** Filesystem suitability; **SQLite WAL and locking correctness**; durability and recovery; **sufficient free capacity against the D135 floors**; sustained-write practicality; and **safe separation from retained D130 evidence**. Every item, not a majority. **The currently attached SSD must NOT be assumed suitable until a future D136 proves it** — and D135 touched no SSD at all: not benchmarked, not written to, not deleted from, not moved on, not reformatted, not repartitioned, with D130 archive evidence unmodified. |
| **What monitoring a future corrected run should carry** | **Six planning-only points — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §11 (D135-R7).** `PRE_LAUNCH` **refuses to launch** below `185` GiB (no partial start, no *proceed and watch*); `POST_F0` and `PRE_F1` **stop and report** at `60` and `55` GiB; `POST_F1_PRE_F2` **refuses F2** below `50` GiB — the existing [Decision 127](Decisions/decision_127_m3_3_pre_f2_admission_guard.md) guard shape with a corrected constant, already failing closed; `DURING_F2` alerts at `20` GiB and **hard-stops at `10` GiB (D124-R5, unchanged)**, and **because F2 is a single transaction a stop is a ROLLBACK — the operator must be told that explicitly**; `POST_F2` reports only. **Nothing is implemented, and no automatic destructive cleanup is proposed anywhere.** |
| **What D135 does NOT establish** | **Anything semantic, and any permission — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §12 (D135-R8, D135-R9).** It establishes a capacity-planning model, the floors, and whether current free disk meets the START floor. It establishes **no** certified corrected semantic count, **no** corrected runtime, **no** complete-source semantic correctness, **no** canary acceptance, **no** E0 or network readiness, and **no permission to execute anything**. The `19,034,205` planning accession universe and every related quantity remain **`CAPACITY_PLANNING_CARDINALITIES_ONLY`** — sized to plan a disk, not certified counts — so **D129-R2's rejection of every D128 count stands entirely** and **D129-R8 still requires any later canary to run from scratch, in a new world, under a new run identity**. |
| **What D135's own limitations are** | **Six, stated rather than smoothed — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §15.** The model rests on **one complete-source run, and that run was defective**; Model B, its only independent anchor, is a **`<0.7%` prefix and a demonstrated understatement**. **No F0 or F1 interior breakpoint exists anywhere in the evidence**, so `F0_PARSE_PEAK` is `UNKNOWN` and bounded only from above. **Peak temporary and spill usage was never measured at scale** — the model carries `0` because `0` is what the evidence supports, **not because `0` is known to be right**. The **D131 deferred-shard reopen phase has never been measured at full scale**. `1 Hz` sampling understates extrema. And **the floors are planning values, not measured requirements**. |
| **What D135 changes about the blockers and the sequence** | **It supplies the capacity model and moves the blocker, rather than clearing it — [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §§13, 18 (D135-R6, D135-R10).** The corrected complete-source canary **remains unauthorized**, now pending **successful external-volume qualification and adoption of the resulting capacity/path architecture**. The **D131 runtime configuration is unchanged** and **D134's mmap and checkpoint candidates remain rejected**. **E0 remains unauthorized**, **network remains disabled** at both tracked switches at request ceiling `0`, and **`census_orchestrator.py::_parse_bulk` remains the independent PRE-NETWORK blocker**, deliberately unrepaired — its repair **must not be performed as a side effect of unrelated work**. **Modelling what a run would need is not authorizing the run.** |
| **Whether the external SSD D135-R5 named was actually qualified, and what the verdict is** | **Yes — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §§4–8, verdict `D136_EXTERNAL_SSD_PASS_WITH_ARCHIVE_ISOLATION_REQUIRED`.** [Decision 135](Decisions/decision_135_m3_3_corrected_run_capacity_reconciliation.md) §10 (**D135-R5**) ruled the attached SSD **must not be assumed suitable** until a future D136 proved it against six requirements. D136 proves all six mechanically. **The verdict is conditional, not unconditional**, on exactly one failing predicate — *no unresolved filesystem-level durability blocker* — because the filesystem is **non-journaled ExFAT** and the **uniquely retained** D130 archive shares its failure domain. **It was NOT softened from a FAIL** (no concrete predicate failed) and it is **NOT INCONCLUSIVE** (the testing was defensible and complete). **Qualification is not adoption.** |
| **Whether an external working root is protected when the volume is NOT attached** | **Yes, and this is what Decision 140 corrects — [Decision 140](Decisions/decision_140_m3_3_total_pre_canary_hardening.md) §4 (D140-R1 – D140-R5).** Decision 138 decided external-or-internal by **device number on the nearest existing ancestor**, which has a wrong answer whenever the volume is absent: `/Volumes/SSK SSD/world` resolves to `/Volumes`, and a **writable directory left at the mount point** resolves to itself — **both classified INTERNAL and ran with no guard at all**. Intent is now read from the **name**: a path under `/Volumes/<name>/` is an external intent whether or not anything is mounted, and **never degrades to internal**. `--require-volume-uuid` is **MANDATORY**; the directory must exist, be **currently a mount point**, and report exactly `397A4D4A-9508-391E-814E-3B533C7BD049`; the volume is **re-authenticated immediately before `mkdir`**; and `create_world` **creates no parent**, so a vanished mount point cannot be recreated on the internal disk and filled. |
| **Whether the shard-to-parent map is proved before a 30-hour run** | **Yes — [Decision 140](Decisions/decision_140_m3_3_total_pre_canary_hardening.md) §13 and §16 (D140-R21).** The D129 parent rule was only resolved **during F0**, ~27 hours in, with a ~120 GiB world already built. A **read-only** structural preflight now runs before `create_world`, traversing the archive once with **the same filters F0 uses** and resolving every governed shard through **`_resolve_shard_parent` itself** — no competing algorithm, filename still corroboration only. **Run on the real source it returned `985,834` governed members, `5,337` shards, `5,337` declared, `0` orphan, `0` duplicate-parent, `0` conflicting-parent, parent map SOUND, shard-before-parent TRUE**, digest `e58b910022aca9c88a2833e50f84efbf0719a86d11c09a5ab99c9e530a7f17eb`, in `108.432` s. |
| **Whether a running canary can be paused and resumed** | **No — [Decision 140](Decisions/decision_140_m3_3_total_pre_canary_hardening.md) §17, returning `D140_PAUSE_RESUME_ARCHITECTURE_REQUIRES_OWNER_REDESIGN`.** Exact mid-F0 resume would require weakening `persist_streamed`'s **create-once** parser-run short-circuit, durable accumulator state with **no schema home below migration `0016`**, and a cursor/DB/sidecar consistency protocol that does not exist — all A1-R20 stop triggers. **There is no `SAFE_TO_EJECT` state**, `kill -STOP` is **not** a governed pause, and the SSD must not be disconnected while a canary runs. The **sound subset** — F0→F1 and F1→F2 boundary recycling, mid-F1 and pre/mid-F2 rollback-pause — is identified and returned for owner redesign. |
| **How the qualified volume is ATTACHED, and whether that is checked** | **It is USB mass storage through a ThinkPad Thunderbolt 4 Dock, and it is now checked mechanically — [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) §4 and §11 (D141-R1 – D141-R5).** `DOCK_TRANSPORT_CLASS = USB_VIA_THUNDERBOLT_DOCK`. The dock is a genuine Thunderbolt 4 device linked at `40` Gb/s, but it negotiated **`usb_four` mode**, its **downstream Thunderbolt receptacle is empty**, and `diskutil` reports the volume's own **`BusProtocol = USB`** — so it is **not** called Thunderbolt storage. The cascade `0x8087:0x0B40` → `0x17EF:0x30B6` → `0x17EF:0x30B8` → SSD `0x090C:0x2320` is walked **from the mounted volume's own media upward**, and the check runs inside the composed preflight all three canary modes flow through. |
| **Whether the runbook's "directly connected — no hub" condition was true** | **No, and that is the finding [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) exists for — §3.** Accepted runbook §28e condition 3 asserted a direct connection; the operator's SSD is **three USB hub tiers deep inside the dock**. The condition was not merely unenforced, it was **false**, and it is corrected rather than quietly dropped. |
| **Whether the AC-power and lid guard D140 wrote was ever actually called** | **No — it was implemented, unit-tested, named by the runbook as "checked at launch", and unreachable; [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) §3 and §11 (D141-R9) put it on the production path.** The two reachability mutations M10 and M11 in §14 leave every guard correct and merely stop the preflight calling it — **both are killed**, so the same defect cannot recur silently. |
| **Whether a changed `disk4`/`disk4s2` number can refuse a launch** | **No, deliberately — [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) §5 (D141-R4, D141-R7).** No BSD identifier is frozen or compared; the current one is a momentary IORegistry lookup key taken from the volume that has **already** proved its UUID. The non-refusal is asserted at both unit and production-envelope level. The **Volume UUID remains the primary identity and is unweakened**. |
| **Whether the D136 direct-connection topology is revoked** | **No — [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) §16 (D141-R8), preserved entire by [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §5 and unchanged by [Decision 144](Decisions/decision_144_m3_3_d143_finding_correction.md) §12.** Both qualified topologies are recognized and never confused; an unqualified third refuses. **Selecting ONE topology for the real canary was the owner's call and D141 did not make it**; [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §4 made it, and [Decision 144](Decisions/decision_144_m3_3_d143_finding_correction.md) §4 (D144-R1) is what makes the production path demand it. **The narrowing is a FIRST-CANARY ENVELOPE RULE, not a revocation**: `QUALIFIED_TRANSPORT_CLASSES` still holds both classes, `require_qualified_transport` with no requirement still admits direct, `require_external_envelope` STILL ADMITS DIRECT when nothing narrows it, and the mechanism is **symmetric** — demanding direct refuses a dock attachment identically, so neither class is privileged by the code and only the caller chose one |
| **What the dock I/O numbers are, and the one reading that had to be re-measured** | **Sustained `388`–`405` MiB/s, EXCEEDING the direct baseline — and the first reading is reported rather than tidied away, [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) §7.** The first write into the new directory measured `110.435` MiB/s, which would have read as a ~56% regression against D136's `250.438` MiB/s. It was **not reproduced**: 2/3/6 GiB runs measured `352.763`, `404.909` and `388.493` MiB/s with a `~420`–`435` MiB/s plateau and no cliff. **Its cause was not isolated and no explanation is asserted.** These numbers may **not** be used to extrapolate complete-source runtime. |
| **Whether charging and SSD I/O actually coexist through the dock** | **Yes, measured DURING the load rather than inferred — [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) §9.** `13` samples across `7.7` s of sustained 4 GiB writing at `530.08` MiB/s: `ac_power` True on every sample, `mounted` True, `st_dev` constant, a **`92` W** adapter connected before, during and after. `DOCK_LINK_FLAPS = 0`, `SSD_DETACH_EVENTS = 0`, `IO_ERRORS = 0` across `63,970` kernel log lines. |
| **Whether qualifying the dock makes it safe to unplug** | **No — [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) §12 and §17, and runbook §28f.F.** No surprise-removal, power-loss or physical-disconnection test was performed or is authorized; `F_FULLFSYNC` returning success is an **OS-visible result only** and is not proof the dock or bridge honoured a flush. **Governed pause/resume remains `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` and there is no `SAFE_TO_EJECT` state — qualifying the dock does NOT create a way to detach it.** |
| **Whether Decision 140 is owner accepted** | **Yes, for continuation — under `M3_3_D140_CORRECTED_PUBLICATION_BASELINE_OWNER_ACCEPTED_FOR_CONTINUATION`, the owner instrument recorded as [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md)'s `ENTRY_BASELINE` and issued BEFORE Decision 141.** It accepts the **corrected** publication baseline — Decision 140 as repaired at `cf9cd34c01e2ede295d562c8eb9f56344247b021`, the HEAD Decision 141 §2 records entering on — **for continuation and no further**. **The acceptance did not come from Decision 141**, which conferred none; D140's own record header still reads `PENDING` as **history**, because it claimed no acceptance when it was published and its acceptance came separately. **ACCEPTED is not LATEST CONTROLLING RECORD, and SUPERSEDED is not REVOKED**: every D140 safety predicate remains inherited and enforced unless a later owner ruling explicitly replaces it. **Decisions 137 and 138 are NOT accepted by this** and remain `IMPLEMENTED — PENDING INDEPENDENT REVIEW AND OWNER ACCEPTANCE` |
| **Whether Decision 141 is owner accepted** | **Yes, for continuation — [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §3 (D142-R1), under `M3_3_D141_OWNER_ACCEPTED_FOR_CONTINUATION`.** The measured and integrated transport class remains `USB_VIA_THUNDERBOLT_DOCK`. **The acceptance is of a BOUNDED qualification and does not widen it**: every D141 §12 limitation is carried forward intact, and acceptance means the record may be relied on as written — never that a bounded measurement may be restated as a general property of the hardware. **Decision 141 is not rewritten, and neither is Decision 140.** Accepting D141 for continuation accepts **neither** Decision 137, 138 **nor** 140 |
| **Which topology the FIRST complete-source canary uses** | **`USB_VIA_THUNDERBOLT_DOCK` — the owner's selection, [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §4 (D142-R2).** This answers the question [Decision 141](Decisions/decision_141_m3_3_thunderbolt_dock_qualification.md) §16 deliberately left open. The selected identity is EXACT: Volume UUID `397A4D4A-9508-391E-814E-3B533C7BD049`, USB vendor `0x090C`, product `0x2320`, serial `SSKPSSD0000000000071`, and the ordered dock cascade `0x8087:0x0B40` → `0x17EF:0x30B6` → `0x17EF:0x30B8`, on **the D141-qualified physical dock port and profile**. **A changed BSD identifier is not a refusal** — `disk4`/`disk4s2` are attach-time state, and the UUID plus the frozen transport profile are the identity (D141-R4, D141-R7 preserved) |
| **Whether `USB_DIRECT` is revoked by that selection** | **No, and it is also not selected — [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §5.** The D136 direct topology **remains separately qualified**; D141 §16 (D141-R8) stands entire. Both facts hold at once: the repository still recognizes two qualified topologies and refuses a third, while the owner has selected exactly one of the two for the first run |
| **Whether a refusing dock preflight may fall back to the direct path** | **No — there is NO automatic fallback and NO operator fallback, [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §6 (D142-R2).** If the selected dock topology fails or refuses its preflight: **STOP**, do not switch that same canary to `USB_DIRECT` silently or manually, do not relax the profile or work around the refusal, and restore the qualified attachment or **return to the owner**. A transport refusal states the physical configuration is not the qualified one; it is never a prompt to select the other qualified one mid-run |
| **Whether governed pause/resume exists for the first canary** | **No — it is DEFERRED beyond it, [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §7 (D142-R3).** `GOVERNED_PAUSE_RESUME = NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, with **no `SAFE_TO_EJECT` state, no governed detach, no governed reconnect, no topology switch, no storage migration and no pause-and-move workflow**. **`kill -STOP` is not a governed pause**, **closing the lid is not a governed pause**, and **qualifying the dock did not create permission to detach it**. The first canary runs under the existing continuous-attachment model with every existing mechanical launch predicate still applying, and **no pause/resume implementation and no new state machine is authorized**. The [Decision 140](Decisions/decision_140_m3_3_total_pre_canary_hardening.md) §17 architectural finding remains open owner work. This is a deliberate freeze, not a claim that pause/resume would never be useful |
| **What happens if a run must be detached or reconfigured anyway** | **It is INTERRUPTED, not PAUSED — [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §8.** An interrupted run is lost and requires a new run identity; worlds are create-once and are never resumed (D129-R8). **There is no procedure for recovering from a physical disconnection, and none may be written** — a documented recovery that does not exist is worse than an honest statement that none does |
| **Whether `--require-volume-uuid` is optional** | **No — it is MANDATORY, and the runbook that said otherwise is corrected: [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §9 (D142-R4).** Accepted [Decision 140](Decisions/decision_140_m3_3_total_pre_canary_hardening.md) §4 (D140-R2) made the assertion mandatory on every external route and made **omission the refusal**; runbook §28d carried the opposite claim in **two** places, and both are corrected because the defect is the meaning, not one sentence's wording. **This was a DOCUMENTATION defect only** — the shipped code has enforced the rule since D140, refusing with an explicit `D140-R2` message before the volume is consulted. The corrected wording: mandatory for the external-SSD canary envelope, **omitting it is a refusal**, and supplying it **does not disable or weaken any other launch guard**. **The exact UUID requirement is preserved**, it is **not** weakened into a mount-name check, and it is **not** made optional through any other CLI, configuration, or API path. **Decision 140 is not rewritten to repair a runbook sentence** |
| **Whether further pre-canary architecture changes are planned** | **No — the architecture is FROZEN, [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §10 (D142-R5).** The owner plans no additional pre-canary architecture changes, which is the confirmation D141 §19 was waiting on before a final review could be commissioned. The intended next phase is a **FINAL INDEPENDENT PRE-CANARY REVIEW**, **not authorized by that record**, to be performed later from a genuinely fresh session context against the exact published D142 commit |
| **Whether a canary phase can end in one process and continue in another** | **Yes, and it is the ONLY governed way to reclaim its RAM — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §§5 and 7.** Both inter-phase boundaries of the first canary, `F0 → F1` and `F1 → F2`, are `QUALIFIED_MAJOR_RESTART_BOUNDARY`; one OS process runs each phase, through `--mode phase-f0|phase-f1|phase-f2`. This is exactly the subset [Decision 140](Decisions/decision_140_m3_3_total_pre_canary_hardening.md) §17 proved sound and returned for owner redesign, and nothing wider |
| **How many major execution phases the first canary has, and what they are called** | **Three — `F0`, `F1`, `F2` — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §§3–4.** F0 parses the one governed source, F1 is the Decision 012 resolution pass, F2 is the Decision 094 §6.4 association projection. The names are the accepted Decision 135 §11 capacity vocabulary and are not invented; world creation and the result document are **not** additional phases |
| **Whether a phase restart is a pause, or makes the SSD safe to unplug** | **No, to both, and this is the most important thing not to misread — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §6.** The restart right exists ONLY after durable terminal success. `GOVERNED_PAUSE_RESUME = NOT_IMPLEMENTED` and `SAFE_TO_EJECT = NOT_IMPLEMENTED` are unchanged; the volume must stay attached to the selected topology throughout, and a restart grants **zero** physical-detach rights |
| **What happens if a phase is interrupted rather than finished** | **It leaves no checkpoint, and its successor refuses — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §§7 and 14.** A checkpoint is written only at durable terminal success, so its presence is the completion proof and its absence is the refusal. A crash, kill, OOM, disconnect or closed lid mid-phase is an **interruption**, governed exactly as [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §8 already governs one |
| **Whether committed rows prove a phase completed** | **No, deliberately — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §13.** A populated world, a working catalog holding every row F0 wrote, and a ledger reading `parsed` are all insufficient without the terminal checkpoint. That exact case is tested: the checkpoint is deleted, every durable row is left behind, and the successor refuses |
| **Whether a restart can reach a topology the owner did not select** | **No — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §11.** `FIRST_CANARY_REQUIRED_TRANSPORT` is now passed at **four** production seams rather than three, so a qualified `USB_DIRECT` attachment is refused at every phase boundary. That matters most there: a boundary is exactly where an operator answering a dock refusal by re-plugging directly would try again. `USB_DIRECT` is still **not revoked** |
| **What free-space floor admits a phase that is CONTINUING a run** | **The accepted floor for the phase about to begin, never the launch floor — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §10.** `PRE_F1` (55 GiB, D138-R6) before F1 and the Decision 126 §7 pre-F2 gate (50 GiB) before F2. The 185 GiB launch floor asks whether there is room to run the whole canary from nothing, which is false by construction once F0 has written. **Not one floor is invented, moved, or relaxed** |
| **Whether the phase sequence changes what a canary produces** | **No, and it is measured rather than asserted — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §18.** The phased result document differs from a whole run in exactly the fields **two whole runs** differ in — run id, two timestamps, two free-space readings, and the working catalog's file digest — and in no others. Every accepted identity, count and totality is identical |
| **Whether Decision 145 authorizes the canary** | **No, and building the mechanism is not permission to use it — [Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §15.** All three activation constants remain `None`, both network switches remain `false`, migration head remains `0015`. The future canary authorization must explicitly authorize the multi-process sequence |
| **Whether Decision 145 was independently reviewed, and what that review concluded** | **Yes, and it FAILED — [Decision 146](Decisions/decision_146_m3_3_final_independent_post_d145_precanary_review.md).** Verdict `D146_FINAL_INDEPENDENT_POST_D145_PRECANARY_REVIEW_FAIL`, **0 BLOCKER / 1 MAJOR / 2 MINOR / 7 OBSERVATION**, against the frozen tree `1ab21d76913e367d972b024c5c1fb006160d52b6` at `HEAD` `69a73d99a2aa5aafeb905d3fcfd40dba6f88e68d` — **not** the later documentation-only commit that publishes it. **Nine of the ten review axes are substantiated**: the process boundary, the durable checkpoint, fresh-process reauthentication, topology, exactly-once, interruption, authority, network and whole-run equivalence all hold. **The review repairs nothing** and **accepts nothing** — not Decision 145, and not Decisions 137, 138, 141, 142, 143 or 144 |

It folds ten frozen constants plus `disclosure_drift.__version__` — the literal `"0.1.0"`, touched by exactly one commit in the entire history. **Proved by mutation**: relaxing the accepted `POST_F0` floor `60 -> 1` GiB and deleting the admission-side predecessor-status guard leaves the digest bit-identical, and **no git SHA, source digest or build identity exists anywhere in `src/`**. The digest IS sound for the constants it binds; the claim drawn from it is not |

[Decision 145](Decisions/decision_145_m3_3_governed_major_phase_restart.md) §12 says "the four capacity floors" |

| **Whether the host execution lock being released between phases is safe** | **Yes, as an `ACCEPTABLE_LIMITATION` — [Decision 146](Decisions/decision_146_m3_3_final_independent_post_d145_precanary_review.md) §8.** Five interleavings were traced: an intruder in the gap can make this run's successor **REFUSE**, but cannot silently corrupt, duplicate or advance it. Three properties carry it — the world is create-once and named by `run_id`, capacity is re-measured inside the lock at every phase, and advancement is gated on a durable checkpoint rather than on anything that merely exists. The residual is operator governance, which is a runbook matter |

Governance-only mode selection was judged sufficient because `--mode run` **cannot damage an in-flight phased run** — every world-creating mode refuses at `create_world` on that identity. `--mode run` is not removed |
| **Whether a phase restart NOW mechanically prevents continuing under changed governing CODE** | **Yes — [Decision 147](Decisions/decision_147_m3_3_d146_finding_correction.md) §§4–7 closes [Decision 146](Decisions/decision_146_m3_3_final_independent_post_d145_precanary_review.md) MAJOR-1.** The owner sustained the finding and chose the harder remedy: *implement genuine mechanical repository continuity; do NOT merely weaken Decision 145's claim*. `phase_execution_identity()` now folds the **git HEAD commit** and the **git HEAD tree** of the repository the running source was imported from, derived by `repository_identity.py` (contract `m3.3-canary-repository-identity/1`) from Git — never declared. Both halves are recorded because an amend moves the commit and not the tree. Proved against **real** temporary repositories with real commits, and end to end in three real OS processes: F0 at commit A, a governing tracked file committed as B, F1 refusing in its own process naming both SHAs |
| **Where the repository identity comes from, and whether an operator can supply one** | **From the executing module's own location, and NO — [Decision 147](Decisions/decision_147_m3_3_d146_finding_correction.md) §4.** The current working directory is never read. The repository Git finds is then **authenticated**: `canary_phases.py` and `single_source_canary.py` must be tracked files of it, so an installed copy sitting inside an unrelated repository cannot yield a real identity of the wrong code. No parameter, no CLI flag and no environment variable can declare a revision — a declared identity would make the contract a statement of intent rather than a measurement. Every Git invocation is a read: exactly `rev-parse`, `status` and `ls-files`, proved from the module's own AST |

The gate runs **first**, before the work root, the volume, the dock, the power state or the host lock. **A refusal is terminal and is NOT a resumable pause** — nothing is checked out, stashed, reset, cleaned, fetched or repaired |
| **How many capacity values the execution identity binds, and which** | **Six, named rather than counted — [Decision 147](Decisions/decision_147_m3_3_d146_finding_correction.md) §8 closes [Decision 146](Decisions/decision_146_m3_3_final_independent_post_d145_precanary_review.md) MINOR-1.** The three admission floors (`LAUNCH` 185 GiB, `PRE_F1` 55 GiB, `PRE_F2` 50 GiB) plus `POST_F0` (60 GiB, a post-phase invariant), `F2_ALERT` (20 GiB) and `F2_HARD_FLOOR` (10 GiB). `WORKING_CATALOG_CACHE_BYTES` stays excluded on [Decision 119](Decisions/decision_119_m3_3_cache_bound_persistence_and_prefix_diagnostic.md)'s evidence-neutrality proof, and that exclusion is itself tested. **No value was invented and no accepted value changed.** A test fails if a seventh capacity constant is ever added unclassified |
| **Whether the stale Decision 126 rationale in the source was corrected** | **Yes, both sentences — [Decision 147](Decisions/decision_147_m3_3_d146_finding_correction.md) §9 closes MINOR-2 and OBS-5 together.** The `PRE_F2_MINIMUM_FREE_BYTES` docstring now separates the two rationale sentences Decision 145 made false, marked **HISTORICAL**, from the two that carry `D126-R6`, marked **CURRENT AND BINDING**. **[Decision 126](Decisions/decision_126_m3_3_complete_first_source_final_preflight.md) is not rewritten**, the gate did not move, and the 50 GiB value is unchanged — the dispositive check still runs in the **F2 process**, inside its own `write_containment`, one statement before `_f2`, proved from the AST |
| **What became of the seven Decision 146 observations** | **All seven explicitly disposed — [Decision 147](Decisions/decision_147_m3_3_d146_finding_correction.md) §10.** OBS-1 (no working-catalog file digest at attach) and OBS-2 (lock-serialized create-once rather than `O_EXCL`) accepted, with their boundaries now **asserted by tests** rather than assumed; OBS-3 closed with a focused `migration_head` test and no architecture change; OBS-4 (the unreachable admission-side status guard) **kept** and pinned; OBS-5 closed with MINOR-2; OBS-6 carried forward as a future authorization boundary with `--mode run` **not** removed; **OBS-7's correction REFUSED on evidence** — an `argv[0]` condition in `process_is_live_canary` would make *alive* harder to detect and could admit a successor while its predecessor was still writing, which is the dangerous direction |
| **Whether Decision 147 authorizes the canary** | **No — [Decision 147](Decisions/decision_147_m3_3_d146_finding_correction.md) §14.** It is a mechanism correction. All three activation constants remain `None`, migration head stays `0015`, both network switches stay `false`, and a passing preflight still prints `canary_authorized: false`. It confers **no acceptance** on Decision 145, on Decision 146, or on any earlier record, and **the independent re-review of this correction must be done by a session that did not write it** |

All three activation constants remain `None`, both network switches `false`, migration head `0015` with `0016` absent. **No file under `src/` or `tests/` is touched by the record** |

| **Whether the SELECTED first-canary topology is mechanically enforced** | **Yes, since [Decision 144](Decisions/decision_144_m3_3_d143_finding_correction.md) §4 (D144-R1) — and it was NOT before, which is [Decision 143](Decisions/decision_143_m3_3_final_independent_precanary_review.md) §18 MAJOR-1.** [Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §4 selects `USB_VIA_THUNDERBOLT_DOCK` and §6 forbids automatic **and** operator fallback; the narrowing mechanism existed and was correct, and **no production caller supplied it**, so all three entry points left `required_transport` at its `None` default and **a directly attached qualified SSD passed the whole envelope** — including as the answer to a dock refusal. The correction is **one constant and three call sites**: `FIRST_CANARY_REQUIRED_TRANSPORT` in `single_source_canary.py`, passed by `run_single_source_canary`, `run_single_source_prefix_profile` **and** `run_canary_source_command`. **It is a module constant and deliberately not an operator input** — no CLI flag, configuration key, or environment variable supplies, widens, or disables it, and **no fallback of any kind was introduced**. Proved through the production entry points, plus an **AST pin** requiring every envelope call in the module to carry it, because MAJOR-1 was an ABSENT argument at every call site at once |
| **Whether the operator runbook accurately states what the application checks at launch** | **Yes, since [Decision 144](Decisions/decision_144_m3_3_d143_finding_correction.md) §5 (D144-R2) — and NOT everywhere before, which is [Decision 143](Decisions/decision_143_m3_3_final_independent_precanary_review.md) §18 MAJOR-2.** Runbook §28f.C presented **eight** rows under *"each is checked by the application rather than by reading this page"*, and two failed that claim: **Transport** (the application checked membership in the two-element qualified set, not equality with the dock class) and **Co-tenancy** (the application checks nothing, as §28f.E of the same section says). It is now split into **§28f.C.1 mechanically enforced launch predicates**, each with the ruling that enforces it, and **§28f.C.2 operator rules the application checks none of**. **Transport appears in C.1 only because D144-R1 made it true, and the section says so** rather than quietly promoting the row. **No new co-tenancy enforcement mechanism was invented** — §28f.E did not overstate its coverage; the table was the defect |

The canary module graph **never imports the orchestrator at all** — proved live through `sys.modules` — and the canary's own bulk path is the **different, repaired** `offline_parse._parse_bulk_submissions`. **It remains an OPEN PRE-NETWORK blocker** that must be repaired before any network or live-retrieval authorization, and it was **not** repaired |

| **Whether the Decision 143 findings were corrected, and whether that makes the review pass** | **All seven are closed, and NO — correcting the findings of a failed review is not passing it: [Decision 144](Decisions/decision_144_m3_3_d143_finding_correction.md) §1 and §19.** 2 MAJOR, 3 MINOR and 2 OBSERVATION, **none omitted and none deferred**. | **What the two Decision 143 OBSERVATION findings were, and what became of them** | **Both closed explicitly, one by documentation and one by proof — [Decision 144](Decisions/decision_144_m3_3_d143_finding_correction.md) §8 and §9.** **OBSERVATION-1**: the host execution lock covers `--mode run` only, so a concurrent `--mode profile-prefix` is not mechanically excluded and would consume space the running canary's capacity model assumes it alone consumes.
