[Decision 142](Decisions/decision_142_m3_3_precanary_architecture_freeze.md) §9 corrected exactly this meaning in two runbook places and could not reach this one, because the text lives in `src/`. **The internal Decision 116 path was not weakened by accident**: the flag stays optional at the parser, because an internal root needs no assertion and never did, and the refusal belongs where externality is known — the envelope |
| **How the D144 corrections were falsified** | **Five reversible, source-isolated mutations, ALL FIVE KILLED — [Decision 144](Decisions/decision_144_m3_3_d143_finding_correction.md) §13.** The caller stops passing the requirement; the requirement is replaced with `None` at every seam; the selected path accepts the direct topology instead; `required_transport` is ignored **inside** the composed envelope; and the CLI help regresses to pre-D140 optionality. Every mutated file was restored from its exact original bytes and **re-hashed against its SHA-256 baseline after every mutation**, and again at the end. **No mutation survived, and no broad mutation framework was run.** The third is worth reading twice: pointing the constant at `USB_DIRECT` kills the **positive** dock test and the changed-BSD-identifier tests as well as the refusals, which is what proves the suite pins the *selected* class rather than merely that *some* class is demanded |
| **Which exact volume is qualified, and what identifies it** | **`/Volumes/SSK SSD`, Volume UUID `397A4D4A-9508-391E-814E-3B533C7BD049` — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §4 (D136-R1).** ExFAT served by **Apple FSKit** (a user-space framework, not a kernel VFS driver), `/dev/disk4s2` on physical `/dev/disk4`, USB, External, Solid State, `131,072` B allocation block, **SMART Not Supported**. **The stable identity is the Volume UUID, never the BSD identifier** — `disk4s2` is assigned at attach time and changes across reboots and re-plugs, which is why D136-R11 item 2 makes a **UUID-keyed fail-closed guard** a D137 requirement. No rejection criterion was triggered: it is local physical storage, not network, SMB/NFS/WebDAV, cloud-backed, or remote user-space storage. |
| **Whether external free capacity meets the D135 floors, and whether the floors moved** | **Yes, with `104.1743` GiB to spare, and the floors did NOT move — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §5 (D136-R2).** Post-cleanup free **`310,498,557,952` B / `289.1743` GiB** against the START floor `198,642,237,440` B / `185` GiB. Headroom is `141.4218` GiB at the projected peak live footprint and `161.4302` GiB at the projected final durable footprint. Three pre-write and three post-cleanup observations each had a spread of **`0`** bytes, and **no archived byte is counted as free**. The contrast with D135-R4's internal shortfall of `71,795,462,144` B is a swing of `183,651,782,656` B. **The `185` GiB START and `50` GiB PRE-F2 floors remain controlling and unchanged.** |
| **Whether SQLite is actually correct on ExFAT — WAL, locking, and crash recovery** | **Yes, all three, tested rather than assumed — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §6 (D136-R3).** Run in the **project's own environment** (`.venv`, Python `3.12.13`, SQLite `3.53.4` — *not* the system `3.9.5`/`3.35.5`) with **no project code altered**. The accepted D131 pragmas read back exactly: `journal_mode=wal`, `synchronous=2`, `cache_size=-524288`, `wal_autocheckpoint=1000`, `foreign_keys=1`, `busy_timeout=10000`, `mmap_size=0`. **Locking used SEPARATE OS PROCESSES**, not two cursors in one: writer B was refused for its full `2.1759` s bounded timeout and **left no row**, a reader was never blocked (`0.0005` s) with correct WAL snapshot isolation, and B committed in `0.003` s after release — **no double writer**. **Crash recovery is correct both ways**: `500` uncommitted rows **absent** after `SIGKILL`, `500` committed rows **survived** a kill with a `24,752` B uncheckpointed WAL and were recovered by a **fresh process**, with `integrity_check == ok` throughout. |
| **How the crash-recovery signalling was contained** | **Ten checks authenticating PID *and* command line immediately before each signal — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §6.** Positive PID; matches the spawned child; not self; not parent; not init; `ps`-confirmed live; PID agreement; parent-PID is this process; command line carries the unique D136 marker; command line is the expected disposable script. **No escalation ladder was used and no project or canary process was signalled** — only disposable D136 children created for the test. This is the containment discipline any future signalling work should copy. |
| **What the I/O numbers are, and what they may NOT be used for** | **`250.438` MiB/s fsync-complete against a `50` MiB/s floor — and they may NOT be used to extrapolate runtime — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §7 (D136-R4).** The `2` GiB file was non-sparse with matching write and read-back hashes; reads ran at `322.438` MiB/s; `0` I/O errors. The bounded **synthetic** SQLite workload (**D134 not reconstructed, no SEC JSON parsed, no canary run**) built `793.96` MiB in `4.176` s with commit latency median `0.0388` s / p95 `0.0544` s / max `0.0741` s, WAL high-water `8.927` MiB, **`0` stalls over `5` s**, and clean integrity checks. **The `50` MiB/s floor is a qualification predicate, not a performance target**, and clearing it `5×` licenses no runtime claim. **One reading trap:** a flat WAL sample is **not** absence of checkpointing — SQLite reuses the WAL in place; the proof it ran continuously is that the high-water held at `8.927` MiB **while `793.96` MiB was written**. |
| **Whether ExFAT allocation forces the `185` GiB floor up** | **No — external overhead is SMALLER than the internal overhead the floor was derived on — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §8 (D136-R5).** Measured external ratios are `1.000000` for the `2` GiB file and `1.0000443` for the `793.96` MiB database, against the internal APFS D128 world ratio **`1.0002757`** that D135's Model A used. Rescaling adds only `6,073,630` B durable and `7,024,938` B peak — absorbed thousands of times over by the surplus. **No floor is raised and no adjustment is proposed.** The counter-risk is recorded rather than dismissed: ExFAT's `128` KiB minimum makes **many small files** expensive (an `8,192` B database allocated `131,072` B — `16×`), bounded here because the run world is a few very large objects — **`853,404` files would be needed to consume the surplus** — but **`SQLITE_TMPDIR` behaviour is UNMEASURED** and carries to D137. |
| **What D136 does NOT establish about durability** | **Power-loss safety, and everything adjacent to it — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §9 (D136-R6).** D136 establishes **process-crash recovery only**. It does **not** establish power-loss safety, surprise-removal safety, USB-bridge cache-flush correctness, or journaled-filesystem semantics. **ExFAT has no metadata journal**, so a loss during a metadata update can damage directory structure, not merely an in-flight file — and `SIGKILL` exercises **SQLite's** WAL recovery, never the filesystem's metadata recovery. **The `~0.9` ms `F_FULLFSYNC` return is explicitly NOT evidence of a physical flush**: it is equally consistent with the USB bridge silently ignoring the cache-flush command, and D136 cannot distinguish the two without power-cut testing. **SMART is Not Supported**, so there is no drive-health telemetry at all. |
| **Whether the D130 archive was disturbed, and why it constrains the verdict** | **Untouched — and it constrains the verdict because it is UNIQUELY retained there — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §10 (D136-R7).** The `~104` GB pax archive was **not opened, not hashed, not re-hashed**; only the compact proofs [Decision 130](Decisions/decision_130_m3_3_d128_archival_and_reclamation.md) §6 (D130-R2) records were verified, in `0.040` s, and the postcheck found **zero differences across all `24` entries** with the archive directory **never written**. The D136 scratch tree was a **sibling at the volume root**, never underneath. But `d130_post_deletion_proof.txt` shows **no internal copy exists**, so a future active database and the archive would share **filesystem, physical device, cable and USB bridge, and surprise-removal failure domain** — which is exactly why an unconditional PASS was unavailable. |
| **Whether the SSK SSD may now host an active database, given D125-R4** | **Only ONE canary, only after D137, and only on that exact UUID — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §10 (D136-R8).** [Decision 125](Decisions/decision_125_m3_3_external_archival_and_reclamation.md) §11 (**D125-R4**) — *the SSK SSD remains cold/archive storage only, no active governed SQLite use, no reformat* — **REMAINS THE GENERAL RULE**. D136's own bounded synthetic disposable tests did not violate it. The owner creates **one narrow exception**: after successful **D137** implementation and acceptance, volume UUID `397A4D4A-9508-391E-814E-3B533C7BD049` may host **ONE** corrected M3.3 complete-source canary working world, as a **separate sibling tree**, with **no write inside `/Volumes/SSK SSD/FDD_M3_3_D130_D128_ARCHIVE`** and **the D130 archive immutable**. It authorizes **no** arbitrary active governed SQLite use and **grants no authority now**. |
| **On what basis the shared failure-domain risk was accepted** | **Five stated grounds, for one disposable run only — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §10 (D136-R9).** Internal capacity **cannot** satisfy the accepted D135 floor (a `66.8647` GiB shortfall against a container where the floor is `81.04%` of total capacity); **every** bounded mechanical SQLite predicate passed; the MacBook battery substantially reduces ordinary mains-loss exposure; the run stays **disposable** until accepted; and **no reformat or repartition** will endanger the archive beforehand. **This ruling does not convert ExFAT into a journaled filesystem and does not claim zero risk** — it is a bounded, auditable acceptance of a named risk. |
| **What physical conditions any later canary run must satisfy** | **Twelve, all required — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §10 (D136-R10).** External power; SSD connected throughout; hardware stationary; no manual eject or unplug; no unrelated write-heavy SSD activity; no reformat or repartition; **D130 archive precheck immediately before launch**; **stable volume-UUID verification**; `>= 185` GiB / `198,642,237,440` bytes free at launch; working root **outside** the archive tree; **D130 archive postcheck after the run**; and sleep prevented for the duration via the governed launcher and runbook. **These are future run conditions only — no run starts under D136.** |
| **What Decision 137 must implement before any canary can be considered** | **Nine items — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §11 (D136-R11).** External working-root selection using an existing supported surface if one exists, otherwise **the smallest new surface necessary**; a **fail-closed identity guard keyed on the stable Volume UUID, not `disk4s2`**; **refusal if the working root is inside the D130 archive tree**; a `>= 185` GiB launch floor; a `>= 50` GiB PRE-F2 floor; **replacement of the known-inadequate `30` GiB `PRE_F2_MINIMUM_FREE_BYTES` behaviour**; D135-R7-consistent phase-boundary and F2 monitoring with the `DURING_F2` hard floor **unchanged at `10` GiB** and the operator told that **a stop is a rollback**; operator/runbook power and `caffeinate`/no-sleep requirements; and **targeted tests with independent validation**. **D137 does NOT start the canary.** |
| **Which D135 row-uplift numerator is authoritative, and how a wrong one was caught** | **`21,098,678` — and the wrong value was in the authorizing packet, not the repository — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §3.** The D136 packet asserted the locked numerator was `21,099,278` and directed a hard stop if the repository showed `21,098,678`. **The premise was inverted.** All four required sources carry `21,098,678`: Decision 135 §4, `capacity_model_locked.json` (SHA-256 `cba5ba2b…b69a982`, **matching the digest D135 itself published**), `model_a_d128_uplift.json`, and the accepted `21`-quantity dual-path cross-check. Three proofs settle it: the packet's **own** stated `U_rows ≈ 1.3189484` equals `21,098,678/15,996,591` (the alternative gives `1.3189859`); the numerator is **derived**, `u = n + h` from measured `15,996,591 + 5,102,087`, so the alternative would need a different **measurement**; and re-running the accepted `bc` cross-check reproduces **every** published total exactly while the alternative reproduces **none**. **The blocking token was correctly not returned** — it names a mismatch between publication and locked model, and none exists. Recorded as an **OWNER PACKET TYPO**; **no repository correction is required or authorized**. |
| **What D136 does NOT do** | **It qualifies a disk; it adopts nothing and authorizes nothing — [Decision 136](Decisions/decision_136_m3_3_external_ssd_active_volume_qualification.md) §16.** It does not adopt the external volume as the working root, change path configuration, add external-root support, edit `PRE_F2_MINIMUM_FREE_BYTES`, change the `185` GiB or `50` GiB floors, create or authorize migration `0016`, authorize E0, network, SEC or HTTP (request ceiling remains `0`), repair `census_orchestrator.py::_parse_bulk`, certify any semantic count, or discharge D129-R2, D129-R8 or D129-R12. It claims **no** power-loss safety and extrapolates **no** canary runtime. **Selecting a path, qualifying a volume, and adopting it are three distinct steps — D136 completes only the second.** |
| **Whether the D136-R11 items are implemented, and whether that authorizes a canary** | **Implemented; and no — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §1, §15.** All nine items ship: external working-root selection, a fail-closed **Volume-UUID** identity guard, D130 archive refusal, the `185` GiB launch floor, the `50` GiB PRE-F2 floor **replacing the `30` GiB constant in code**, phase-boundary and continuous capacity monitoring, operator/runbook conditions, and targeted tests. **It carries NO acceptance token and authorizes NO canary.** The D136-R8 exception takes effect only after D137 implementation **and acceptance**; this is the implementation. A passing preflight prints `canary_authorized: false`. |
| **Whether D137 created a second root-selection mechanism** | **No — the existing `--work-root` was sufficient and is reused unchanged — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §3, §5.** The canary already enforces the work-root invariant twice, through one primitive: at the operator surface for a fast refusal, and inside `run_single_source_canary` as the run's own invariant. **What was missing was never a way to *name* an external root but a way to *authenticate* one** — `SQLITE_TMPDIR` returned **zero** repository-wide matches beforehand. The guards live in one new module and reach the run through **one optional parameter**; omitted, every path behaves exactly as Decision 116 left it. |
| **What identifies the qualified volume in code, and what does not** | **The Volume UUID alone — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §4 (D137-R1).** `require_qualified_volume()` compares against `397A4D4A-9508-391E-814E-3B533C7BD049`, case-insensitively and otherwise exactly. **`disk4` and `disk4s2` are attach-time identifiers and are never identity**, and the mount path is not either — `/Volumes/SSK SSD` is whatever volume happens to be mounted there. Wrong UUID, absent volume, and a failed lookup are three causes with **one outcome: refusal**, with **no fallback to internal storage**. Identity is read from `diskutil info -plist` as a **property list**, never the human-oriented table, and the mount point is **derived** by an `st_dev` walk because `diskutil` will not answer for a subdirectory. |
| **How the D130 archive is kept out of the working root, including under aliasing** | **Three refusals on `realpath`-resolved, case-folded path components — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §6 (D137-R3).** The root may not **be** the archive, lie **inside** it, or **contain** it. Comparing components is load-bearing in both directions: `realpath` collapses `..` and follows symlinks before anything is compared, so aliasing cannot get in, and a component-wise prefix cannot mistake `FDD_M3_3_D130_D128_ARCHIVE_WORKING` for a child of the archive, so **a lawful sibling is not falsely refused**. A naive `startswith` fails the second case. |
| **Which capacity floor applies where, and whether raising one raised another** | **Three floors, three questions, and none replaces another — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §8, §9.** `PRE_LAUNCH` `>= 185` GiB / `198,642,237,440` B **refuses to launch**; `POST_F1_PRE_F2` `>= 50` GiB / `53,687,091,200` B **refuses F2** before its transaction opens; the **continuous** `DURING_F2` floor stays at `10` GiB / `10,737,418,240` B (**D124-R5, unchanged**) with alerting from `20` GiB. **Raising the admission gate to `50` GiB did not raise the emergency floor** — the first says what must be true before the transaction opens, the second what must hold during it. |
| **Whether the superseded `30` GiB pre-F2 behaviour is still reachable** | **No, and it is proved rather than asserted — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §8 (D137-R5).** It was one constant read from one comparison, so raising the constant retires it entirely — there is no second branch, fallback, or caller override. `test_the_superseded_thirty_gibibyte_amount_no_longer_admits` pins free space at **exactly `32,212,254,720`** and requires a refusal. **The constant moved; the mechanism did not** — strict `<`, the call site between F1's return and F2's call (D126-R6), and the Decision 094 §6.4 ordering are byte-unchanged, and **the guard is retained, not removed**. Decision 127's own test file is **updated in place, not deleted and not skipped**. |
| **What a hard stop during F2 actually costs, and who decides it** | **A rollback of the whole in-flight projection, and the operator — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §9 (D137-R6).** F2 is a **single transaction**, so stopping inside it discards the work rather than truncating it, and the watchdog's message says so rather than leaving it to be inferred. The `capacity` subcommand **reports and never acts**: no signal, no deletion, no cleanup at any threshold, and the accepted D131 no-escalation invariant untouched. Its thresholds are **imported** from the package, because a monitor carrying its own copy of a frozen floor can drift from the gate it monitors. |
| **Which capacity phase boundaries a corrected run records, and why `DURING_F2` is not one of them** | **Five in-process, and `DURING_F2` belongs to the watchdog — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §10 (D137-R7).** `PRE_LAUNCH`, `POST_F0`, `PRE_F1`, `POST_F1_PRE_F2`, and `POST_F2` are written into the result document; an invented label is **refused**. F2 is one blocking call inside one transaction, so **nothing in that process can sample it** — claiming it in-process would invent a measurement never taken. **An unknown measurement stays `None`, never `0`**, because a missing WAL reported as zero is indistinguishable from a checkpointed one. A run with no external requirement records nothing and renders no key, leaving the accepted D119 evidence-equivalence byte-unchanged. |
| **Where SQLite spills during a corrected external run, and who sets it** | **An explicit `SQLITE_TMPDIR` on the same qualified volume, set by the operator and validated by the code — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §11 (D137-R8).** Four fail-closed conditions: set and non-blank, absolute and existing, outside the D130 archive, and on the **same qualified external volume** as the working world. **It is validated, never set by library code** — assigning it inside a guard would put a process-wide side effect in a path whose purpose is to refuse unsafe states, and would hide the setting the operator must see. Unset, SQLite spills to the internal volume **silently**, which the capacity model does not cover. |
| **How the D130 archive is checked around a future run without hashing `104` GB** | **The four D130 §6 compact proofs, with the tar `stat`-ed and never opened — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §13 (D137-R10).** The same bounded check Decision 136 §10 ran in `0.040` s. A **precheck** difference refuses the launch; a **postcheck** difference is a **blocker**, because it would mean the corrected canary disturbed the only surviving copy of the D128 evidence. That the tar is never read is proved by a **tripwire over `Path.read_bytes`**, not by reading the source. |
| **How strongly the D137 guards are actually tested** | **`17` reversible mutations, every one caught by named tests — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §17.** Each protection was broken or inverted in place, the targeted tests were run, and the file was restored, with the tree verified byte-identical afterwards; **no source-mutating framework was used**. The focused suite went from **`317` passed** before any edit to **`384` passed** after — `317 + 65 + 2`, with no regression. |
| **What the live external preflight established, and what it did not** | **Every mechanically checkable condition, read-only — and not the physical ones — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §18, §12 (D137-R9).** UUID **exact match**; archive present with **`24` entries** and **no differences** in `0.171` s; free **`310,498,951,168` B / `289.1747` GiB** against the `185` GiB floor, surplus `104.1747` GiB; archive-child, volume-root, unset-`SQLITE_TMPDIR`, and internal-`SQLITE_TMPDIR` controls all **refused**; **nothing created on the volume**. Free space is `393,216` B — three `131,072` B ExFAT allocation blocks — **higher** than D136 §5 recorded, stated rather than rounded away. **Eight of the twelve operator conditions cannot be verified mechanically**, and the runbook says the preflight's silence about them is **not** evidence they hold. |
| **Whether D137 claims anything new about ExFAT durability** | **No — D136's boundary is preserved exactly — [Decision 137](Decisions/decision_137_m3_3_external_working_root_and_capacity_safety.md) §14 (D137-R11).** Nothing implemented claims journaled filesystem semantics, **power-loss safety**, **surprise-removal safety**, or **USB-bridge cache-flush correctness**. Decision 136 §9 established **process-crash recovery only**. These guards reduce **capacity and path** risk; they do not convert the volume into a journaled one. |
| **What the D137 independent review concluded, and whether D137 is accepted** | **Correction required — three majors, zero blockers — and NO, D137 was never owner accepted — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §1, §3.** The accepted verdict is `D137_INDEPENDENT_REVIEW_CORRECTION_REQUIRED`. **MAJOR-1**: the external envelope could be bypassed by omitting the optional flag. **MAJOR-2**: the `DURING_F2` `10` GiB floor was classification and reporting only. **MAJOR-3**: the D135 `POST_F0` and `PRE_F1` gates were never enforced. Decision 138 corrects the **implementation**; **Decision 137 remains historical and is not rewritten to pretend the defects never existed**, and neither record carries an acceptance token. |
| **Whether the external safety envelope can be skipped by leaving the flag off** | **No — the resolved work root decides, not the argument — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §5 (D138-R1).** `external_volume_candidate()` classifies the root by **device number on its nearest existing ancestor**, before any world exists and with **no `diskutil` call for an internal root**; `require_external_envelope()` then runs the **complete** D137 envelope for any external root, flag or no flag. **An unclassifiable root refuses** — internal is the only answer that admits without proof. `--require-volume-uuid` is an **assertion that can only add a requirement**: it forces the envelope onto a root that would classify internal, and asserting it for an internal root refuses at the identity guard. **Omitting it cannot disable a single guard.** |
| **Which external volume identities D138 authorizes** | **Exactly one, and it creates no generic authorization — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §5 (D138-R12).** The expected identity handed to the envelope is the frozen `397A4D4A-9508-391E-814E-3B533C7BD049`, **never the caller's string**, so an arbitrary asserted UUID is refused **before anything is measured** and an external root on any other volume fails for the corrected external-canary mode. **D125-R4 remains the general rule** outside the narrow D136-R8 one-canary exception, and D138 does not widen it. |
| **Whether the D130 archive can be reached with the flag omitted** | **No — archive exclusion is unconditional on the qualified volume — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §5 (D138-R2).** It was always inside the envelope; the correction is that the envelope is **always entered**. The refusal itself is byte-unchanged D137-R3 — `realpath`-resolved, case-folded **components**, so `..`, a symlink, and a case variant cannot launder containment **and a lawful sibling sharing the name prefix is still not falsely refused**. Live, with no flag supplied, the archive child, the volume root, and an unqualified external root were **all refused**; under Decision 137 every one of them was an admission. |
| **Which environment the `SQLITE_TMPDIR` guard actually validates** | **The one SQLite consumes — the process environment — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §5 (D138-R3).** The guard resolves the value from `os.environ`, which is the only environment the spilling process has, and an explicitly supplied mapping must carry the **identical** value; a disagreement is a **refusal**, not a preference. Validating one mapping while SQLite reads another proves nothing about where the spill lands, which was the D137 review's MINOR on this guard. The normal CLI path supplies no mapping and stays deterministic. |
| **How the `DURING_F2` `10` GiB floor is actually enforced** | **From inside the process running F2, aborting inside its open transaction — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §6 (D138-R8).** `F2CapacityGuard` raises `F2CapacityHardStopError` while the transaction is still open, so `transaction()` **rolls it back**: the in-flight projection is **discarded, not truncated**, and **no partial F2 association state commits**. `> 20` GiB is normal; `<= 20` GiB records a `DURING_F2` alert and **continues**; `<= 10` GiB aborts; **a capacity-measurement failure takes the same abort path**, fail-closed. Both thresholds stay **inclusive** and neither moved. **No thread, no background process, no signal, no deletion, no escalation** — D131 is untouched — and the guard binds **only** on the protected external path. |
| **How often F2 is sampled, and why that is not a boundary check** | **From F2's own per-accession loop in both traversals, on a monotonic clock — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §6 (D138-R9).** One reading is taken **before** the transaction opens and readings continue **while** it is open. The interval is bounded by wall clock rather than iteration count: `5` s scheduled against a `60` s ceiling, so a long batch cannot buy hours of unobserved execution, and a per-accession call stays affordable because most calls return immediately. A **monotonic** clock is used so a system time adjustment cannot silently suspend sampling. |
| **What survives the F2 rollback, and how the rollback is proved** | **Bounded evidence carried on the exception, and a row count observed inside the transaction — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §6 (D138-R10), §9.** The hard-stop record is built **before** the raise and held **on** the exception — phase `DURING_F2`, reason, measured free bytes or the measurement-failure class, threshold, observation time, authenticated volume, and explicit `f2_transaction_rolled_back: true` / `f2_committed: false`. Writing it into the transaction being rolled back would destroy the record the operator needs. **The proof is not that an exception was raised**: the capacity provider reads the association row count on the **same connection** from inside the open transaction, watches it **climb from zero**, breaches at the first non-zero count, and the table is **`0` afterwards** — with a control run that commits, so an empty table proves a rollback rather than a projection that never wrote. |
| **What the watchdog `capacity` subcommand is for now** | **Supplemental operator observability, and nothing more — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §6 (D138-R11).** Enforcement that depends on a human starting a second process is not enforcement, which was MAJOR-2. **Exit `6` does not stop F2** — the in-process guard is what stops it — and the module's prose, exit-code documentation, and subcommand help all say so. It still sends no signal, deletes nothing, and cleans nothing at any threshold. |
| **Which capacity floor applies where, after the correction** | **Five floors, five questions, and none replaces another — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §7 (D138-R4 – R7).** `PRE_LAUNCH` `>= 185` GiB **refuses to launch**; `POST_F0` `>= 60` GiB / `64,424,509,440` B and `PRE_F1` `>= 55` GiB / `59,055,800,320` B each **stop and report before F1 begins**; `POST_F1_PRE_F2` `>= 50` GiB **refuses F2** before its transaction opens; the continuous `DURING_F2` floor stays at `10` GiB with alerting from `20` GiB. **`>=` admits at every floor and one byte below refuses**, an **unmeasurable boundary refuses**, and **nothing is deleted or cleaned to clear any of them**. `PRE_LAUNCH` and `POST_F1_PRE_F2` keep their own accepted call sites and are **not moved**; the `30` GiB behaviour remains unreachable. |
| **Why `POST_F0` and `PRE_F1` are two gates rather than one** | **They answer different questions, and phase-boundary verification is the point — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §7 (D138-R6).** *Did F0 leave enough behind?* and *is there enough to start F1 with?* Folding them would lose the verification that having both provides. It also has a testing consequence worth knowing: because the floors **descend** — `60`, then `55`, then `50` — **no single free-space value can breach a later gate without having breached every earlier one first**, so each gate is reached in isolation only by dropping free space **between** boundaries, which is also the real shape of the failure. |
| **How strongly the D138 corrections are tested** | **`13` reversible mutations, every one killed — and two test weaknesses found by falsification rather than by design — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §9.** The focused suite went from **`384` passed** (the exact accepted D137 count, reproduced before any edit) to **`450` passed** — `384 + 66`, no regression — with ruff, `ruff format --check`, and **mypy strict over all `94` source files** clean. Every mutated file was verified byte-identical by SHA-256 afterwards and **no source-mutating framework was used**. The two weaknesses are recorded because that is what falsification is for: an entry-reading test asserting on reading *count* could not distinguish *before F2 opens* from *at F2's first accession* and now asserts on `in_transaction`; a sampling control asserting only `samples > 1` survived removing either traversal's call and now asserts the **shape** of what the readings observe. |
| **What the D138 live preflight established** | **That the envelope activates without being asked — read-only, with nothing created — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §10.** UUID **exact match**; archive **`24` entries, no differences** in `0.133` s with the `103,966,696,960`-byte tar **`stat`-ed and never opened** under an `open` tripwire; free **`310,498,951,168` B / `289.1747` GiB**, byte-identical to D137 §18, clearing the `185`, `60`, and `55` GiB floors. **Four no-flag controls all refused** — external root, archive child, volume root, arbitrary asserted UUID — and an internal root with no flag returned `None`. **Nothing was created, written, deleted, or benchmarked, and no canary world exists.** The **accepting** path remains proved **synthetically**, because creating `SQLITE_TMPDIR` is a write. |
| **How many of the twelve operator conditions are mechanically verified** | **Five, not four — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §11.** Decision 137 §12 states *"four of the twelve are mechanically verified and eight are not"*; the runbook table it describes marks **five** as preflight-verified — the volume UUID, the archive precheck, the `185` GiB floor, archive isolation, and `SQLITE_TMPDIR` placement — leaving **seven** to the operator and the launch command. `Docs/m3/operator_runbook.md` §28d now states the correct counts. **Decision 137 is left byte-unchanged**; the erratum is recorded in Decision 138 rather than corrected in D137's history. |
| **What Decision 138 does NOT authorize** | **Any canary, and everything downstream of one — [Decision 138](Decisions/decision_138_m3_3_d137_safety_envelope_correction.md) §14 (D138-R13).** No canary world, run identity, execution namespace, launch receipt, or terminal state; no corrected complete-source canary and no canary of any kind; no E0; no migration `0016`; no network at request ceiling `0`; no adoption of the external volume; and no certified semantic count. **It claims no owner acceptance, for itself or for Decision 137.** The next authorized action is an independent review of this correction. |

*(Current state: **the PRE-E0 chain is closed and execution is authorized.** The Decision 101 E0
invocation was **interrupted**; Decision 103 implements the successor generation and the governed
stale-lease recovery, and authorizes **implementation only** — reconciling the real lease and
running E0 v2 each still require a separate owner instrument. Decision 104 makes that boundary
executable: the recovery activation constant ships `None`, so the surface it implements is
**disabled as shipped**. Decision 105 closes the last acceptance issue on that implementation — an
existing lease the reader cannot account for now refuses at both ordinary gates instead of reading
as permission — and grants no authority of its own. **Decision 106 then owner-accepts that whole
implementation chain at BLOCKER 0 / MAJOR 0, publishes it, and authorizes exactly one read-only
real-state preflight** — acceptance of an implementation, never activation of an operation. That
preflight ran and measured `L1`–`L11` **PASS**, and **Decision 107 owner-accepts the measurement and
authorizes exactly one real reconciliation**: the recovery constant carries the D107 token for that
single use and returns to `None` afterwards, and `M3_3_E0_EXECUTION_AUTHORITY` is set to `None`
**first**, so clearing the lease cannot re-enable E0-v2 as a side effect. That reconciliation was
executed exactly once, verified, and owner-accepted, and **Decision 108 is the separate owner
instrument E0-v2 was awaiting**: §2 (R120) authorizes exactly one real execution and §3 (R119)
withdraws the spent transition grant at the same time, so E0 is the *only* execute surface open and
its own §5 (R122) shuts it again as soon as the one invocation has returned. Network, SEC, and HTTP
authority remain **NONE** at request ceiling **0** throughout, the ordinary authorized pushes
aside.)*

## Deviation register — where deviations are recorded

**[`Docs/preregistration.md`](preregistration.md) §25 is the canonical preregistration deviation
register.** It states the fields every deviation must record and holds the register itself.

- **Deviation D001** is currently the only entry: the cohort-assignment date-source rule and
  point-in-time boundary frozen by
  [Decision 010](Decisions/decision_010_temporal_availability_and_cohort_assignment.md), recorded as
  prospective, outcome-blind, and made before any filing was retrieved.
- **Accepted decision records may explain, justify, or approve a deviation**, and several do — but a
  decision record is not itself the register.
- **The preregistration section remains the register of record** unless a later accepted decision
  explicitly replaces it. None does.

`make cohorts` prints §25 among the governing records for the frozen cohort definitions, so the
register is reachable from the CLI as well as from this index.

## Full chronological registry

For every decision's exact status, supersession relationships, and date, use
[`Docs/Decisions/decision_registry.md`](Decisions/decision_registry.md) — it is the authoritative,
chronological record. This file is a topic-oriented convenience index over the same underlying
decisions and never overrides it.
